100%;font-size:0.66em;font-style:italic;color:#000;margin-top:0.5em;text-align:center">PLATE IX — THE FIGURE IN ONE LINE, TEN STEPS, NO PEN LIFT. Red is the stroke being made, black is what is already drawn. The triangle closes first (1–3); the circle is then run counterclockwise from 9 all the way to 1, leaving one 40° arc open (4–6); the line is drawn (7–9); and the last stroke is that same open arc, 1→9, which closes the figure on the point it began at (10).</div></div>

**The execution above, written out, is 9–3–6–9, then counterclockwise all the way round to 1 — which leaves exactly one 40° arc undrawn — then the line 1–4–2–8–5–7–1, then that last arc 1→9.** Nineteen points, eighteen edges, none
repeated, closed on the nine it started from.

**How many ways there are: 8668.** At each of the nine points the pen
arrives and leaves twice, so it must pair the four edge-ends into two
pairs. There are three ways to do that: let the circle run through and
the chord run through (**T**), or switch between them one way (**X**) or
the other (**Y**). Nine points, three choices: 3⁹ = 19683 schemes in
all. Of those, **8668 draw the whole figure as one closed curve.** The
count is confirmed independently by direct enumeration: there are 34672
closed edge-sequences starting from point 9, and a single curve through
a four-valent point offers two places to cut and two directions, so 8668
× 4 = 34672.

| the scheme yields | 1 curve | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| number of schemes | **8668** | 8264 | 2473 | 270 | 8 |

**Least action applied to the drawing gives 72 minimal schemes: going from three curves to one requires at least two switches, and two are enough.** Choosing **T** everywhere
gives exactly three curves — the circle, the triangle and the line, each
closing on itself, which is the figure as it is usually *seen*. Every
point switched away from **T** changes the number of curves by at most
one, and each first switch by exactly one. The 72 minimal schemes sit at 18 switch-locations with four schemes each,
and the 18 are exactly {a triad point} × {a hexad point} = 3 × 6. The
circle has to be joined to the triangle at a point of the triangle, and
to the line at a point of the line. That is the whole of the
connect-the-dots freedom.


**The eighteen minimal switch-pairs and their seventy-two executions,
stated explicitly.** Every minimal execution is fixed by naming its two
switch points — one on the triangle, one on the line — and then choosing
X or Y at each, which is what decides the direction the pen takes
through them. The full connect-the-dots catalogue:

| | on the line → | 1 | 2 | 4 | 5 | 7 | 8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **on the triangle ↓** | **3** | 3·1 | 3·2 ◊ | 3·4 ◊ | 3·5 | 3·7 | 3·8 |
| | **6** | 6·1 | 6·2 | 6·4 | 6·5 ◊ | 6·7 ◊ | 6·8 |
| | **9** | **9·1 ◊** | 9·2 | 9·4 | 9·5 | 9·7 | 9·8 ◊ |

Each cell is four executions — X or Y at each of its two points — so 18 × 4 =
72. The six marked ◊ are the pairs that are neighbours on the circle, where the
arc joining the two switch points is a single 40° arc; the other twelve leave a
longer way round. **9·1** is the one in bold: neighbours, containing the origin,
containing the seed.

**By the same three principles as the two-stroke case, the best of the 18 is {9, 1}.** Six of the 18 are neighbouring points on the circle, so the arc between
them is one arc rather than a detour. Two of those six contain the origin 9.
One of those two contains the seed 1. Its virtue is visible in the drawing: the arc left open and the last stroke made
are the same 40°, because the opening lies exactly between the two switch
points.

**What the counting object is.** Pairing the four ends at a four-valent
point in one of three ways is the same local choice a knot diagram makes
when a crossing is smoothed: leave it, or resolve it one way or the
other. The tally of how many schemes give one curve, two, three and so
on is the figure's tally of transition systems by component count, the
circuit-partition data of a four-regular graph. The Martin polynomial
proper is taken over the transitions consistent with an Eulerian
orientation, and its coefficients depend on the orientation chosen. The
enneagram drawn in one line is a state of the unrestricted tally, and
the 8668 is its count at a single component. That is the mathematics the
count belongs to; any reading past it has not been earned here.

**One coincidence is recorded and given no weight.** The minimal schemes
number 72. The number is fixed by the shape alone, 3 triad points × 6
hexad points × 2 × 2, so any figure built this way would give 72. Its
agreement with the proton's station is exact, unexplained, and carries
no weight. It is stated here so that it is not over-read. The same
applies to the earlier totals. The hexad's points sum to 27 and all nine
to 45. But the units of any n sum to n·φ(n)/2, and 1 through n to the
triangular number, so both are what nine gives and not what the figure
gives.

## 18 · The grading of the nine, and the color of matter

### The question: why the count of colors is three

The strong interaction counts three colors. In the Standard Model that count
is an input. The ratio R of hadronic to muonic cross-sections in
electron–positron annihilation measures it, and the neutral pion's decay
rate confirms it; the gauge group SU(3) is chosen because three is what the
instruments return. Nothing in the received construction says why the answer
is three rather than two, four, or seven. The question is old, standard, and
open.

The Origin on Discrete Terms, a companion record, arrives at the same
count from one side. The electric charge table of a fermion generation
is solved there as a four-constraint system, and a scan over the
multiplicity m finds that only m = 3 lands the solution on the
third-integer lattice. At m = 1 the system is sterile; m = 2 demands
quarters; m = 9 demands ninths. That result selects three, but by
inspection of alternatives rather than by a structure that admits no
alternative. This chapter supplies the other side: a derivation of the
threefold structure of the ring of digit sums, Z/9, with no scan over
the multiplicity. Four choices precede it and are named where they
enter: the modulus, which is base ten's; the proper quotient over the
identity; the additive structure over the multiplicative; and the
doubling order over the positional. The identification of the structure
with color charge is made below and is not derived.†

<div class="vnote">† Version 3 said the derivation makes no scan and no
selection. It makes no scan; the four choices named here precede
it.</div>

### Theorem: the figure admits exactly one grading

The residues of the figure are the group Z/9. That group has exactly
three subgroups — {0}, {0, 3, 6}, and Z/9 itself — the subgroup chain of
every cyclic group of prime-power order. It follows that Z/9 has exactly
one proper nontrivial quotient, the map Z/9 → Z/3 that reads each
residue by its value modulo three; its quotients in all are of orders
one, three and nine. The multiplicative group of the figure's units,
cyclic of order six, has a threefold quotient of its own, whose fibers
are the three Midy pairs {1, 8}, {2, 7}, {4, 5}. The additive quotient
is the one taken here, and the choice is one of the four named above.

Written in the figure's digits, with 9 standing in the place of 0, the
kernel of that map is the triangle {3, 6, 9}. This settles two
properties of the figure that the accounts known to the author notice
and do not derive. The triangle is closed under itself, and the
six-point line never touches it, because the triangle is the kernel of
the figure's one quotient — a subgroup, and the complement of the line
by construction. The two familiar facts are corollaries of kernel-hood,
not observations about a drawing.

The consequence for any physics carried on the figure is immediate. A
charge structure that is a proper nontrivial coarsening of the figure's
residue arithmetic has threefold multiplicity, and its neutral class is
the triangle; the identity quotient, a ninefold grading with the single
neutral residue 9, is the only other. Given the proper quotient there is
no parameter to tune: the multiplicity is the subgroup lattice of the
modulus.†

<div class="vnote">† Version 3 said any charge structure on the residue
arithmetic has threefold multiplicity or none. The identity quotient is
ninefold; the statement holds of the proper nontrivial quotient.</div>

### Corollary: the three classes are the fibers of the one quotient

The quotient's fibers are {1, 4, 7}, {2, 5, 8}, and {3, 6, 9}: every digit
belongs to the class of its residue modulo three. The figure's digit
arithmetic respects the classes throughout, since digit sums preserve
residues modulo nine and therefore modulo three. The three classes are one
object — the fiber structure of the unique quotient — and every later
statement in this chapter is a statement about them.

### Theorem: the orbit alternates between the charged classes and never reaches the neutral axis

The six-point line, read as the doubling orbit 1 → 2 → 4 → 8 → 7 → 5,
carries the classes 1, 2, 1, 2, 1, 2. Every doubling step exchanges the two
charged classes, because multiplication by two exchanges the residues one
and two modulo three. And the orbit never reaches the neutral class: the
line and the triangle partition the nonzero digits.

A reading is attached to this theorem, and it is stated as a reading. If the
charged classes are carriers, then a free carrier is never neutral. A bare
charged state cannot present itself to observation — not because a wall
confines it, but because the carrier's own dynamics alternates between the
two charged classes and contains no move that reaches the neutral class
alone.

### Theorem: the minimal neutrals are the meson and baryon patterns

Consider combinations of parts drawn from the charged classes, asking which
reach the neutral class. With one part, none does. With two parts, exactly
one combination does: one part from each charged class — an opposite pair.
With three parts, exactly two combinations do: three parts of the first
class, or three parts of the second. Nothing else exists at or below three
parts.

The minimal neutral combinations of the grading are therefore a two-part
opposite pair and a three-part same-class triple. These are the meson
pattern, the baryon pattern, and the antibaryon pattern, obtained here by
residue arithmetic alone, with no reference to any gauge group.

### The rounding maps the charged classes onto the axis, with the reading at the quantum level

The Origin's first volume establishes the commitment ladder: the three
clean rounding depths of the register, which lift the carrier digits 2,
8 and 5 to 3, 9 and 6. Placed inside the grading, that mechanism reads
as follows: the register's own rounding lifts one charged class onto the
neutral triangle. The class {2, 5, 8} is lifted by one at the rounding
depths three, four and five and lands on {3, 6, 9}; the class {1, 4, 7}
is committed unchanged at depths one, two and six.†

<div class="vnote">† Version 3 said the rounding maps the charged
classes onto the triangle. It lifts one of the two; the other commits
where it stands.</div>

The reading at the quantum level is stated as the reading it is.
Commitment is the register's word for what measurement, condensation and
mass acquisition share, and the landing says that what is lifted lands
neutral. Read onto the color sector: observability requires the neutral
class. The grading states which combinations can be neutral; why charged
states do not occur asymptotically is a dynamical question, outside this
chapter. The free carrier never presents (the alternation theorem), what
presents has landed (this subsection), and the states that present are
the minimal landings (the theorem on minimal neutrals): the hadron
families as the register's shortest routes to the triangle.

### The structure matches the center of SU(3), and two independent derivations reach three

What the received theory calls the center of SU(3) is the subgroup Z₃ of
phases commuting with the whole group, and triality is the charge that
sector carries. Quarks sit at triality one, antiquarks at two, and the
adjoint at zero, with confinement the statement that only color singlets
appear asymptotically, of which triality zero is a necessary condition.
The abelian part of that structure, two charged classes exchanged by
conjugation and a neutral class, is the structure established above;
observability confined to the neutral class is the clause the
identification adds.

Two derivations, each conditional on its stated input, now reach the
same multiplicity. The first is this chapter's: the subgroup lattice of
the modulus admits one grading, and it is threefold. The second is the
Origin's: the charge table's conservation constraints, solved at
multiplicity m, land on the third-integer lattice only at m = 3. Group
structure on one side, linear algebra on the other, one answer between
them — and the physical count, measured by R and by the neutral pion,
standing on the third side.

What remains open is stated with it. The non-abelian dynamics remains to
be derived: the adjoint's dimension and the Casimir values are matched
observations, and the running of the coupling and asymptotic freedom lie
outside this chapter. The center is derived, conditional on the
identification below; the group is matched.

### The identification: color charge as the figure's one grading, stated as such

This chapter makes one identification: color charge is the figure's
unique grading. Equivalently, triality is the register's class modulo
three, with quarks the {1, 4, 7} reading, antiquarks the {2, 5, 8}
reading, and the observable sector the triangle. It is an
identification, of the same epistemic kind as the constraint system that
yields the charge table, and not a theorem.

Under it, the results assemble with no further freedom: the multiplicity is
three, the neutral class is the triangle, single charged states never
present, and the minimal presenting states are the meson and baryon
patterns.

### What the identification forbids: a fourth color, off-lattice charges, a free quark

The identification is exposed to measurement at three points, and a failure
at any one of them refutes it outright.

1. **No fourth color.** Z/9 has no subgroup of index four and no
  quotient of order four, so a color count of four is impossible under
  the identification. The count is measured directly: the cross-section
  ratio R in electron–positron annihilation and the neutral-pion decay
  rate both scale with it, and both fixed the count at three decades
  ago. The condition is met in retrospect, and it is stated so that the
  identification is seen to carry it. The charge-table scan agrees from
  its own side: m = 4 demands eighths, off the lattice.

2. **No charges off the third-integer lattice.** Held by the scan's
  arithmetic of the charge table; the grading supplies a Z/3 label and
  not a charge value, so this condition tests the Origin's construction.
  Any confirmed quark charge away from an exact third refutes it.†

<div class="vnote">† Version 3 said the condition is held twice over, by
the scan and by the lattice of the grading. The grading gives the class
modulo three and no charge value.</div>

3. **No observable free quark.** A charged-class state presenting
  asymptotically contradicts the identification's clause that the
  observable sector is the triangle. The record of fractional-charge
  searches, from the levitated-niobium reports of 1977 and 1981, which
  were not reproduced, through the automated oil-drop searches to the
  collider programs, holds no confirmed observation in half a century
  [LaRue, Fairbank and Hebard, Phys. Rev. Lett. 38, 1011 (1977);
  Particle Data Group, free-quark searches].

### Verification: every arithmetic statement pinned in the public suite

Every arithmetic statement in this chapter is pinned within the
programme's suite: the subgroup lattice, the fibers, the alternation and
the untouched triangle, the exhaustive enumeration of minimal neutrals,
the rounding's landing, and the multiplicity scan. The grading is pinned
in `verify/test_the_triad_grading.py`, the landing in
`verify/test_the_triad_landing.py`, and the partition of line and
triangle in `verify/test_enneagram_line.py`. The readings attached to
the alternation theorem, to the rounding and to the identification pin
nothing; they are labeled where they stand.


## 19 · The forms as diagrams: one-loop orderings and color flows

This chapter reads the sixty forms through two standard objects of quantum field theory: the orderings of the legs of a one-loop
diagram, and the color flows of an amplitude with three quarks and three antiquarks. The counts and the algebra are exact. Whether
the correspondences reach from structure to dynamics is open.

**Result 19.1. The sixty forms are the sixty orderings of six legs around one loop.** A one-loop diagram with six external legs is
fixed, as far as its ordering goes, by the cyclic order in which the legs attach to the loop. Up to rotation and reversal there are
(6 − 1)!/2 = 60 such orderings, the number of closed forms. Reversal is Furry's pairing of the loop's two orientations, whose
contributions add when the number of legs is even.

**In dual coordinates, closure is conservation of momentum.** In planar diagrams each external momentum is the difference of two
points that lie between adjacent legs. Read the digits, by their logarithms to base two modulo nine, as those points, and each step
of a form as an external momentum. Every form's momenta then sum to zero modulo six. The legs that carry the half-turn momentum 3,
the one momentum equal to its own negative, are exactly the form's complement chords.

**Result 19.2. Three cross-ratios identify every class.** On the doubling hexagon, where the figure's symmetries act as rotations
and reflections, the squared chords are 1, 3 and 4. Each form's three hexagon cross-ratios are the same for every member of its
class, and no two classes share them. Every value is a ratio of powers of two and three.

| representative | cross-ratios | representative | cross-ratios |
|---|---|---|---|
| 124875 (matter) | 9/16 · 9/16 · 9/16 | 124785 | 1/4 · 1/4 · 16/9 |
| 127548 | 9 · 9 · 9 | 125478 | 1/9 · 1/4 · 1/4 |
| 124578 (convex) | 9/4 · 9/4 · 9 | 125748 | 1/9 · 4 · 4 |
| 142857 (rest) | 1/16 · 1/16 · 9/16 | 125847 | 1/4 · 9/4 · 16 |
| 145827 | 1/4 · 1/4 · 9 | 127458 | 1/9 · 1 · 1 |
| 128547 | 4 · 4 · 9 | 124587 | 4/9 · 1 · 4 |

**Result 19.3. A form winds about the hexagon's center in a definite way exactly when no leg carries the half-turn.** Each half-turn
leg is a diameter and can be read as +3 or −3. Without such legs the winding is defined: the doubling form, rest's class and the
class of 124785 wind once, and the class of 125847 does not wind.

**The six-point case is the first in which cross-ratios carry information.** In planar gauge theory the amplitudes of four and five
legs are fixed by dual conformal symmetry, and the six-point amplitude is the first to depend on the hexagon's cross-ratios, through
the remainder function from two loops. There the points are separated by light-like intervals; here they sit on a regular hexagon.
The correspondence is one of structure.

**Theorem 19.4. With §18's identification, the six alternating forms are the color-flow basis.** Read residue one as quark and
residue two as antiquark. For three colors the color singlets of three quarks and three antiquarks span six dimensions, spanned by
the six flows that connect each quark's color to one antiquark's. Read through its left-out pairing, each alternating form is one
of these flows, and the six are independent.

**Each class's overlap with the singlet pairing is three to the power of its closed color loops.** The singlet pairing joins each
quark to its own nine-complement. Matter overlaps it by 27, three loops; the convex class by 9, two loops; the class of 127548 by 3,
a single loop through all six legs.

**The baryon-antibaryon structure is the signed sum of the alternating forms.** The color tensor ε_ijk ε^lmn equals the six flows
summed with the sign of their pairing: matter and the class of 127548 enter with plus, the convex class with minus. The character
table of the symmetric group on three letters turns the three alternating classes into the three color channels of the system,
symmetric, antisymmetric and mixed, of dimensions one, one and four.

**The residue patterns read as hadronic groupings.** An alternating form is three quark-antiquark pairs, a blocked form a baryon
beside an antibaryon, a mixed form a diquark-antidiquark pair beside a quark-antiquark pair. On this reading the first operation,
which carries the doubling form to rest, is the rearrangement of three mesons into a baryon and an antibaryon, the color structure
of proton-antiproton annihilation into three mesons. Whether this reading extends to rates is open.

### What the diagram reading establishes, and what remains open

**A Feynman diagram draws a term whose value comes from outside it; the forms carry their own arithmetic.** The question this
chapter opens is whether that arithmetic supplies the values as well as the structure. The answer so far divides cleanly.

**Three things are established.**
- *The combinatorics.* The sixty forms are exactly the one-loop orderings of six legs, and the twelve classes are those
  orderings up to the figure's own symmetries.
- *The kinematic skeleton.* In dual coordinates every form closes as conserved momentum, and three cross-ratios identify
  each class.
- *The color algebra.* The alternating forms are the color-flow basis of three quarks and three antiquarks, with their
  overlaps, the baryon-antibaryon structure as their signed sum, and the three color channels.

**Five things remain open.**
- *A rule that gives each form a number from its own steps*, the role a Feynman rule plays. The cross-ratios are
  candidates for the arguments of such a rule; they are not yet its value.
- *Propagators and vertices in spacetime.* The steps are momenta in the cyclic group of order six. Masses, spins and
  couplings do not yet appear.
- *Rates.* The reading of the first operation as the rearrangement of three mesons into a baryon and an antibaryon fixes
  a color structure, not a cross-section.
- *The open classes.* Which, if any, of the mixed classes matches a measured multiquark state is open, and so is the
  physical reading of the symmetries that fix each class.
- *More loops and more legs.* The figure's six units give one loop with six legs; whether other moduli give the other
  orders of the expansion is open.


## 20 · Verification, priority and the terms of refutation

Every exact statement in this volume is machine checked within the
programme's public suite, and the battery that pins each chapter is named
here. The reader with Python can re-derive the volume's entire exact
content in minutes.

- The two orders and the line theorem (§4, §17): `verify/test_enneagram_line.py`, the five parts of the theorem; the
  condition generalized across bases, `verify/test_enneagram_catalog.py`; the one-line drawing and its 8668
  executions, `verify/test_the_execution_of_the_figure.py`.
- Midy and the two families (§5): `verify/test_the_midy_involution.py` and `verify/test_midy_pairs_not_orders.py`.
- The gallery of multiplication (§6): `verify/test_encyclopedia_geometry.py`, which covers the epicycloid family and
  its cusp counts at modulus 720, and the envelope's tangency to the base circle at the fixed points with its cusps
  between them. It covers the convergence of the diagram on the curve at moduli 60, 240 and 960, and the enneagram's
  line in the gallery.
- The ladder of best approximation (§7): the convergent record of log₂3, `verify/test_motion.py`.
- The rings and the scales (§8): the transpositional stabilizers, `verify/test_tonal_function.py`.
- The spectrum of the figure (§9): `verify/test_gravity_form.py` and `verify/test_structure_growth.py`.
- The cyclotomic tower (§10): `verify/test_the_registers_selection_second_pass.py`, where a prime closes at period d
  exactly when it divides Φ_d(b) and no earlier value, and 151 is the unique prime of binary period fifteen.
- The register (§11): the bases in which the construction runs, `verify/test_enneagram_catalog.py` and
  `verify/test_the_line_selects_its_arithmetic.py`.
- The sixty forms and their twelve classes (§12): `verify/test_sixty_forms.py`, `verify/test_the_sixty_forms.py`, `verify/test_the_twelve_classes_of_forms.py`, `verify/test_the_census_of_transforms.py`, `verify/test_the_table_of_forms.py`, `verify/test_class_two_the_cyclic_pairing.py`, `verify/test_the_mixed_forms.py`.
- The transforms of the figure (§13): `verify/test_the_two_faces_in_their_two_moduli.py`, `verify/test_the_census_of_transforms.py`, `verify/test_the_colour_flow_and_symmetry_of_the_classes.py`, `verify/test_the_colour_column_of_the_feynman_table.py`.
- The modulus expanded (§14): `verify/test_enneagram_modulus.py`.
- The five structured forms (§15): the balanced forms as the multiples of 999, `verify/test_sixty_forms.py` and
  `verify/test_enneagram_v4.py`.
- The intonated layout (§16): `verify/test_intonated_enneagram.py`.
- The grading of the nine (§18): `verify/test_the_triad_grading.py`.
- The forms as diagrams (§19): `verify/test_the_forms_as_one_loop_hexagons.py`, `verify/test_the_colour_column_of_the_feynman_table.py`.
- The text of Part I, its cross-references and its notes:
  `verify/test_enneagram_v4.py`.


The count of strokes is classical in substance: the fractions k/p fall
into (p − 1)/ord_p(b) cyclic classes, the cosets of the subgroup the
base generates, in the theory of decimal periods that Gauss's
Disquisitiones Arithmeticae of 1801 completed. The author knows of no
prior statement of that count as the strokes of the drawn figure, or of
the completeness facts assembled in this volume, and states that as
belief.† A citation that corrects it is asked for, and will be
registered in a revised version's change log. The content is refuted by
a prime whose stroke count differs from (p−1)/ord_p(b), one
counterexample being enough, or by an arithmetic error in any pinned
part; each is checkable in minutes. One question is put in earnest, as
mathematics: **is there another diagram, from any tradition scientific
or otherwise, whose complete formal content — states, dynamics,
symmetry, conservation, and a generating operation — is a single closed
line?** The content of the standard model is a Lagrangian of dozens of
terms; a Feynman diagram carries one process; a Dynkin diagram carries a
symmetry but no dynamics. A reader who knows such a diagram is asked to
name it. Part II takes the question up in its technical form (§22).

<div class="vnote">† Version 3 counted the stroke-count characterization
among the statements with no known prior source. The count of cyclic
classes belongs to the classical theory of decimal periods; what is
claimed as new is its reading as the strokes of the figure.</div>

*Here the figure is mathematics, and the mathematics is enough.*


# PART II · THE BURNING QUESTION

## 22 · So is it “all and everything”? The technical criteria of a unitary expression

The introduction to this corpus sets out criteria that any theory claiming to account for everything should meet
[Introduction, §2, p. 2]. The second asks for a unitary expression, "in a sense, a symbol", that is symbolic and also "contains
the means of unpacking, in a technical sense, the basis of the theory". It cites the Ouroboros, the Hopi symbol of Life
and the Egyptian Ankh. Conjecture: those figures recorded, for the cultures that made them, matters of fact as those
cultures understood fact. The conjecture concerns symbols as records of knowledge, and the figures alone cannot decide
it.

What the criterion asks of a modern equivalent can be stated without that conjecture. Modern thought takes fact, in
these matters, to be mathematical, so the equivalent must be a figure whose content is mathematics: complete,
non-allegorical, and translatable into decimal mathematics. The question of this section is whether the nine-point
figure satisfies those technical criteria, so that the content of a unified account can be derived from it and not
merely displayed on it. Put shortly: can the Enneagram do it?

The question has a second part, which the first does not settle. It asks whether a theory of everything in the full
sense must also be relatable to the pictures of the world kept by the ancient traditions. If it must, a figure that met
the technical criteria would be the natural place to test that relation. The question falls between fields, and to the
author's knowledge it has not been put in this form. Mathematics treats the figure's components as elementary and
separate. Physics has no criterion under which a figure could count as the expression of a theory. The esoteric
literature that kept the figure asserted its universality and did not seek a proof.

This section constitutes the question rather than answering it. It states the criterion in technical terms, sets out
what an answer would have to contain, reports what is known on each count as of October 2026, and maps the ground for
and against. The verdict is left to the work the last paragraph names.

**A unitary expression is generative, closed and restrictive.** It is generative when the theory's structures follow
from operations on the figure, so that a reader who knows the operations can rebuild them. It is closed when nothing
outside the figure is needed beyond inputs it names, and closure in its strongest form is completeness (§23). It is
restrictive when its structure forbids outcomes, so that a measurement could show it wrong. A diagram that labels the
parts of a theory has none of the three. A Lagrangian is generative and closed for its dynamics but is not a figure,
and a Dynkin diagram carries a symmetry and no dynamics (§20).

**An answer in either direction would have to settle five matters.**

1. *Formal content.* The figure must carry a definite structure that can be stated without interpretation: its
   states, its dynamics, its symmetries, and what it conserves.
2. *Derivation.* Every physical structure attributed to the figure must follow from it. Every choice between
   alternatives along the way must be derived or named as a premise.
3. *Prohibition.* In each domain it reaches, the figure must forbid outcomes. Some of its prohibitions must concern
   measurements not yet made, and some must forbid outcomes that the received theory allows.
4. *Magnitude, read through rest values.* No dimensionless construction yields a dimensionful magnitude. The companion
   record on units proves that once a theory's dimensionless content is fixed, exactly one reference scale is needed
   beyond it [Units, §2, p. 3]. The record on the vacuum proves the counterpart in time: exactly one epoch, the present one
   [Vacuum, §4, p. 3]. In a discrete theory that one scale is the theory's own least count, so the theory determines every ratio
   and every count, and one measurement fixes the dictionary into conventional units; the ratios themselves need no
   measurement. A figure can be asked for every ratio and for the relations among the offsets by which measured values
   depart from their rest values. What remains open is a crossing, not a wall: which object in the world is one
   inscription (§24). The corpus holds a route to it through the atomic mass unit as the structure's native unit, with the
   archetypal nucleon placed at 1.008, and the step that remains open is a ruler-free functional whose minimum is that anchor.
5. *Correspondence.* For the second part of the question, the figure must account in its own terms for why traditions
   that worked with number and cosmos placed the order of the world at nine. The account must be testable against
   those traditions' own statements, and not against resemblances found by an outside reader.

**Magnitude enters through one unit, and a figure can be complete only up to it.** The two theorems fix the inputs
beyond the dimensionless content at one scale and one epoch, and no dimensionless construction can produce either. The
scale is the least count of the theory itself, and the open crossing is the identification of that count with an object
in the world. The arithmetic has the same
shape at its smallest scale. The units record proves that unity read to a finite depth falls short of one by exactly
one unit of the last place [Units, §9, pp. 16–17]. It proves also that the two members of each complementary pair of sevenths, read
to one full period, leave residues that sum to exactly one unit. In general, if m is prime to the base b, the period of
1/m has k places and B is its repeating block, then b^k − m·B = 1. For one seventh in base ten, 10⁶ − 7 × 142857 = 1.

Interpretation: the arithmetic shortfall of one unit and the one reference that a theory must borrow are one fact,
seen in arithmetic and in physics. Both are a single unit that the structure requires and does not contain. The
theorems themselves establish what this section needs: a unitary expression of a physical theory can be complete only
up to one external unit, and that unit is where magnitude enters.

**What a figure can still be asked for is the relation among offsets.** In the corpus's terms a measured value stands at
its rest value displaced by an offset. A ratio of two offsets is a pure number, so it lies inside what the theorems
permit and bears on measured values directly. The companion record on the nucleon masses gives the form such a claim
takes. The proton and neutron masses, in atomic mass units, stand above their rest values by offsets whose measured
ratio agrees with 23/32 to better than one part in a million. The ratio is registered as a constant: a neutron mass that
moves it by three standard deviations, or any drift of it, refutes the account [Proton and neutron, §14, p. 16; Schedule, §11, p. 11].

**The account of each matter below distinguishes three kinds of statement, and every result in it is verified by
computation.** Three kinds occur. A result follows from the figure's arithmetic alone. An identification joins a
structure of the figure to a physical quantity, and is named where it enters. An open result is a correspondence found
exact for a model, whose physical reading has not been decided; each such result carries the sentence that it may or
may not be physics in terms of our current understanding. Keeping the three apart is the discipline that the second
matter demands.

**Part I settles the first matter, and three further structures extend it.** The figure's one closed line carries a state space,
the residues, and a dynamics, the orbits of the period's shift and of doubling. It also carries a symmetry, Midy's
reflection; a conserved structure, the triangle the line never enters; and a generating operation, the involution that
exchanges the two orders. Three further structures are of the same kind. The sixty closed forms on the hexad fall into exactly twelve classes under the figure's arithmetic symmetries, each with its own address (§12). The transforms between forms are twelve, of which the first operation is the only one that respects the mirror (§13). The twelve symmetries of the figure form the automorphism group of the root system of SU(3), the Weyl group of that algebra together with the central reflection (§13). The three inversions are the Weyl reflections and the reversals of the orbit's orientation, and the mirror is the central reflection. The signature of a spatial parity, a central involution of order two distinct from the mirror, is absent from the group. The sixty forms are the sixty orderings of six legs
around one loop, and the six alternating forms are the basis of color flows when the grading of §18 is read as color
(§19). Section 23 shows that the theory of the figure is complete.

**The figure selects its own arithmetic, which answers the question of the base at its root.** The line theorem of
§17 defines a family of figures, one for each prime and base. State the figure's properties without its numbers: one
closed stroke; a stroke as long as the base's digit ring has invertible elements; a stroke that exhausts them, so that
the untouched points are the non-invertible ones; and the mirror. Below base 3000 exactly four pairs of prime and base realize those properties, with digit rings of two, four, nine and 729 elements. The nine-point figure is the smallest with more than two points and the only one in base ten. The complete figures live in bases 3, 5, 10 and 730,
so ten is the least base in which a figure with more than two points can be drawn at all. The numbers 1, 4, 2, 8, 5, 7
are therefore the coordinates that the line admits, and not a choice made before it.

**The second matter is settled in part, and the table states exactly which part.** The table lists what the figure or its
companion records supply for each structure that a complete account must contain, with the premise on which the
supply rests, and what remains open. The structures that follow from the figure's own arithmetic are the charge and color structure of matter, the lock stated below, and the two faces of a long-range sector. So do the three generations as commitment depths, the form of gravitation and the diagram combinatorics. The couplings come from the wider register,
and the dynamics is not yet the figure's.

| structure | what is supplied, and on what premise | what remains open |
|---|---|---|

| color | triality from the unique grading of §18, with four named choices and the identification with color; the color-flow basis from the alternating forms; the junction operator annihilating the singlet and the decuplet | the gauge group and its dynamics are imported |
| the lock | Q + t/3 an integer on every state of the figure, from one premise of color's type together with two choices the record already makes; in the Standard Model the same statement is the Z₆ condition of the gauge group's center | its truth for every physical state, a hypothesis by structure (§23) |
| the two faces | a read face and a circulation face, identified with the electric and the magnetic; a closed circulation has no sources, so no magnetic monopole [Origin, I §6, p. 12; II §2, p. 16] | the identification |
| generations | three, as the three clean commitment depths; a fourth light generation forbidden [Origin, III §4, p. 27] | the identification |
| gravitation, form | one sign, source proportionality and the equivalence principle from one congruence, the inverse square, no central singularity, the coupling 5/(2¹⁵¹ − 1) in ratio form [Gravity, §13, pp. 26–28; §15, pp. 36–37] | the identification of the subdominant's shortfall with the potential |
| gravitation, dynamics | the deficit's linear equation on the count, with inverse square, flux conservation and light's own speed and dispersion; the two words of the metric that make every static, stationary and radiative field exact [Gravity] | the spatial word from counting; the asymmetric two-body field |
| electromagnetism, dynamics | with the count's charge shared between a vertex and its eight cube neighbours, light is a dynamical field with Gauss's law at every tick and Coulomb's law between charges; with the time rounding's erasure the one-loop magnetic moment is a definite number, the same for every mass, with the Schwinger term supplied by momenta near the mass | a lattice-scale correction to g that no mass removes, between a quarter and nine tenths of the Schwinger term by model, excluded by the electron's measured moment; the count whose two chiralities share one momentum is the present count, so the correction is the medium's; under the record's rounding of light the count at rest is a singular point of its dispersion and the rounded vacuum has a channel two thirds of a turn above it; which clock light keeps remains open |

| cosmology | the vacuum fraction on 2/3 with w = −1, the budget in fifteenths, helium at one quarter, the present epoch by theorem [Origin, III §7, p. 29; VII §2, p. 53; Vacuum, §§3–4, pp. 2–3; Registry, §2, p. 3] | the baryon asymmetry; the Hubble tension beyond the registered value |

**The figure fixes a nine-level state space, and its step and clock fix the form that the Born rule reads.** The figure
is not a vector space, but it fixes one, the functions on the residues modulo nine, which is the construction the
wavefunction record uses on any cycle [Origin]. Its step S and clock C obey the Weyl relation CS = ωSC with ω a ninth root
of unity. Doubling is a unitary symmetry of the pair that preserves the hexad and the triangle separately. The
sesquilinear forms invariant under both the step and the clock are the multiples of the counting form alone, by
Schur's lemma. So the quadratic form the Born rule reads on the nine levels is fixed by the figure's own operators up to
one scale. The magnitudes of a given state remain that state's content. The sum of a point and its double lies on the
triangle, at 3 for one triality class and at 6 for the other, and the sum of those two is the rest. So the triangle the
count never enters is the set of relations the count creates, and nine is the least modulus in which the count runs
through every unit while its relations stay off the count. The continuum formalism is the reading of the frames on
their low states. On the tower of cycles of lengths 9, 27 and onward, the Heisenberg commutator holds on the lowest
oscillator states to the precision of the frame. No frame carries it as an identity, and the deficit it leaves is
conserved by the frames exactly.

**The third matter has gained prohibitions that the received theory lacks, each with its refutation.** The Falsification
Schedule carries them with their conditions [Schedule, §4, p. 4; §6, p. 7; §11, p. 11; §19, p. 17; §24, p. 20]. No magnetic monopole. No fourth light generation. No quark charge off a
third. No color-neutral particle of non-integer charge, which is the lock. The vacuum's equation of state exactly
w = −1 at every redshift. No primordial non-Gaussianity of the local kind. The constancy of the nucleon offsets' ratio
23/32. The inverse fine-structure constant not below 137.035999 at zero momentum. The medium's model adds one prohibition
and one requirement. The potential's face rides the trace of light's own step, so gravitational and electromagnetic propagation share one speed and one dispersion at every order. The joint observation of GW170817 and its gamma-ray burst bounds the difference below three parts in 10¹⁵ [Abbott et al. 2017]. The model satisfies that bound, and a measured difference would refute it. And light bends once by a deficit carried in the count alone and twice by one carried in count and address alike. The observed deflection, with the Cassini bound on the parametrized γ at two parts in 10⁵ [Bertotti, Iess and Tortora 2003], therefore requires the address register to carry the deficit. That requirement
may or may not be physics in terms of our current understanding; stated as a requirement, it is what the model needs in
order to stand.

**The registered prohibitions divide by their source into two kinds, and the figure's are all of the first.** An
invariant forbids kinds: what can exist, for every value of the continuous parameters. A dynamics forbids outcomes: a
value, a rate, an epoch or a drift. Both are tested by measurement. Classified by source and by kind, the registered
prohibitions sort accordingly. The four that come from the figure's own arithmetic forbid
kinds: the monopole, a fourth light generation, a charge off a third, a skew of the pair layer. The nine that name a
value come from the register or from the companion records, and none comes from the figure. So the figure's part of the
third matter is complete in its own category, and new prohibitions are the work of the medium, where outcomes occur.

**The corpus settles the fourth matter in ratio form, and the ratio form is a path to magnitude.** Masses in atomic mass
units are ratios to a twelfth of the mass of carbon-12, so a rest value derived in those units is dimensionless and lies
inside what the theorem permits. With the one external reference, the atomic mass unit expressed in kilograms, every
such rest value becomes an absolute magnitude, and nothing further is borrowed [Units, §5, p. 5]. The rest values and the
registered ratio of offsets come from the companion records, and their derivation from the figure itself remains open.
The medium's model adds a ratio of its own, open. Matter's top speed falls short of light's by a term of second order in the ratio of the walker's rest wavelength to the step. That shortfall may or may not be physics in terms of our current understanding. The fifth matter remains open, and §27 describes where it can be tested against a tradition's own
statements. The figure's modern appearance has one author, and his cosmology is the only commentary by that author on these
matters; the provenance makes a technical inquiry into his account a duty of the question, whatever its outcome. A first
test of this kind has been made against a reading of
Gurdjieff's own cosmology, the prime substance of *All and Everything: Beelzebub's Tales to His Grandson*, with the correspondence between its terms and the
figure's fixed before the comparison. The tradition's statements about the three forces, the one irreversible step and the
neutral ground agree in shape with the figure's grading, its rounding and its rest. On four points they do not agree under
the natural identification. The three forces appear separately where the figure's classes do not; the cycle never returns
to its ground where the figure's relations do; and the tradition's two coordinates are independent where the figure's are
coupled. No statement of the tradition was found that the figure predicts and that could have been otherwise. One
candidate is named, the direction of the change each cycle leaves, which the figure fixes by level and the text leaves
open, and reading it at source remains the step this test leaves. The comparison is held with a reservation. No assumption is made that the quantities set against the book's
statements reflect its author's understanding, nor that this reading of the book is correct. The work was made
deliberately obscure, stands alone to the present author's knowledge, and has resisted conventional interpretation.

**Seeking in the figure means running the identification forward.** The usual direction maps known physics onto the
figure. The forward direction asks what the figure itself requires, in five steps.

1. Complete the figure's algebra first: every operation it admits and every symmetry of those operations, so that
   nothing is added later to fit a result.
2. Fix the dictionary by type. Once one identification is made, such as the threefold grading with color, every element
   of the same type is read the same way, and the dictionary cannot be adjusted item by item.
3. Date every consequence before comparing it with measurement.
4. Read the figure's absences as predictions. If the figure is closed, a structure it lacks is a structure it forbids.
5. Treat every choice the derivation made as a rival figure, and compute what each rival predicts. A consequence shared
   by every rival belongs to the figure, and one that changes with the choice belongs to the choice.

**First example: each charged point carries two labels, and neither reading of the second label holds.** The units of
the ring of nine, 1, 2, 4, 5, 7 and 8, are the powers of two and form a cyclic group of order six. The group is the
product of a twofold part and a threefold part. The twofold part's classes are the two charged classes of §18, {1, 4, 7}
and {2, 5, 8}. The threefold part's classes are the Midy pairs {1, 8}, {2, 7} and {4, 5}. Each unit is fixed by its class
and its pair, and each step of the doubling exchanges the class and moves to the next pair. Read as colors, each pair
would be a color with its anticolor, but the octonion picture of the second example places the colors on the
period's steps instead. Read as generations, each pair would be one generation's doublet, but that reading takes the
classes as down-type and up-type quarks and breaks §18's assignment of the classes to quark and antiquark, which the
second step of the method forbids. The example is a negative result of the method, kept as one.

**Second example: the period of one seventh runs on the octonion table.** Label the seven imaginary octonion units by
the residues modulo seven, with the multiplication table given by the seven translates of the oriented line (1, 2, 4),
the standard cyclic table. Multiplying every label by a quadratic residue, 1, 2 or 4, is a symmetry of the product.
Multiplying by a non-residue, 3, 5 or 6, carries the product to its mirror image. Base ten acts as 3, so base ten
reverses the product, and its square, the doubling, preserves it.

The remainders of the decimal period of one seventh, 1, 3, 2, 6, 4, 5, label the six units other than e₀, and the
remainder zero, e₀ itself, never occurs. Each step of the period lies on a line through e₀, alternately a line of the
table and a line of its mirror. Left multiplication by e₀ pairs the six units as (1, 3), (2, 6) and (4, 5). These pairs
are complex coordinates on the six-dimensional space on which the stabilizer of e₀, the group SU(3), acts as a triplet
[Günaydin and Gürsey 1973].

The period walks those coordinates in order. Its steps on lines of the table stay within one coordinate and write the
digits 1, 2 and 5. Its steps on lines of the mirror pass to the next coordinate and write 4, 8 and 7. Each coordinate is
visited once per period, in the order in which the doubling, a symmetry fixing e₀ and therefore an element of that
SU(3), cycles them. Shifting by half the period multiplies every remainder by 10³, which is −1 modulo seven and a
non-residue. Midy's reflection, the figure's mirror symmetry, is therefore the orientation reversal of the octonion
product.

The stabilizer of e₀ is the group that the received construction identifies with color. On that identification the
period of one seventh traverses the three complex coordinates of the color triplet once each. The mirror is not a
symmetry of that group: it carries the table to its mirror twin. The symmetries common to the table and its twin form a
different SU(3), the one fixing e₀ + e₁ + … + e₆, and on that group the mirror acts as charge conjugation. The octonions
thus place the colors on the period's steps, and they do not read the Midy pairs as colors. Each statement in this
example is exact and is checked by direct computation in the verification suite.

**Third example: the figure's mirror computes as charge conjugation, so it yields no prohibition on handedness, and the
lock stands as a hypothesis.** The
figure carries two labellings that its mirror reverses: the direction in which rounding moves the place before, and the
octonion orientation of the period's steps. They agree at every Midy pair except (4, 5), the pair that flanks the empty
antipode. Every labelling that the mirror reverses, however, is the class of §18 multiplied by a function of the Midy
pair, so these labellings carry the class and not an independent handedness. The corpus's own handedness is the exchange
of the two orderings of the hexad, which the gravity record names as the two orientations of the period's screw
[Gravity, §15, p. 37].

Read as a reversal of handedness, the mirror would combine with the gravity record, which places gravity on the
mirror's two fixed places, to forbid gravity any handedness. The mirror does not act so: it acts as charge conjugation
wherever it can be computed, on the classes, on the characters of the ring and on the octonions. Nor does the figure fix
the vacuum angle of the strong interaction at zero. The angle is
unchanged by charge conjugation, and its physical form includes the phase of the quark masses, which the figure does not
carry.

**Hypothesis: every state has electric charge Q and color triality t with Q + t/3 an integer.** This is the condition
that all matter be invariant under the subgroup Z₆ of the Standard Model's gauge group, whose presence current
experiments cannot decide [Tong 2017]. It forbids an isolated particle of non-integer charge that carries no color, such
as one of charge e/6, e/3 or 2e/3, which the Standard Model as an effective theory allows [Koren and Martin 2025]. The
grand unified theories SU(5), SO(10) and E₆ forbid the same states, so the hypothesis is shared with them. On the figure it follows from one premise of color's type, that the thirds of charge are carried by the grading, together with two choices the record already makes. Those are the additive composition of §18 and the identification of the generations with the commitment depths. The premise is stronger than anomaly cancellation and has the content of the unified theories' condition. For every physical state the hypothesis is the step from every instance to the totality, which §23 shows no
complete theory decides from inside.

The figure gives the hypothesis a natural form. The map d ↦ −3d modulo nine sends the quark class to 6, the antiquark
class to 3 and the triangle, which holds every minimal neutral combination, to the root. With one elementary charge
taken as one full turn, these are the places of the quarks' and antiquarks' charges, and every color-neutral state sits
at the root. Searches have excluded such particles of charge 2e/3 between 50 and 640 GeV, and of charge e/3 only between
50 and 60 GeV [CMS 2025]. For e/6 the LHC sets no bound [Koren and Martin 2025]. One confirmed color-neutral state of
non-integer charge refutes the hypothesis, and the next collider data are expected from 2030. As the registry states it, the hypothesis also forbids a millicharge produced by kinetic mixing with a hidden photon, which the Z₆ condition alone permits. Whether the registered form keeps that scope or narrows to the Z₆ condition is decided in the registry and not here.

**Fourth example: the twelve symmetries are exactly the automorphism group of the A₂ root system, and the figure does not
establish the readings of its inversions as parity or CP.** The group is Aut(A₂) = W(SU(3)) × {±1} (§13), and the
signature of a spatial parity is absent from it. The inversions are the Weyl reflections of SU(3) on the figure's weights and spatial reflections with the energy kept on its momenta. A planar figure cannot separate the two readings, since the difference between them is helicity, which the figure does not label. The composition takes
one factor from each dictionary, and a single diagram's relabelling symmetry is not an interaction's violation, QCD
conserving both. Three readings are therefore not established by the figure. They are that the inversions are the spatial
parity, that the mirror composed with an inversion is the combined charge and parity operation, and that a form fixed
only by that composition carries the weak interaction's profile. The counts are exact. Under the mirror and an inversion the sixty forms partition as ten fixed by both, thirty-two fixed only by their composition, six only by the mirror and twelve by neither. No form is fixed by an inversion alone.

**The charted correspondences form one position: the lock, without unification and without monopoles.** The figure's
read face and circulation face are identified with the electric and the magnetic, and a closed circulation has no
sources, so the figure forbids the magnetic monopole, a refutation condition of the origin record [Origin, I §6, p. 12]. The mirror
computes as charge conjugation in every internal dictionary and as the crossing of a line's legs on momenta. Triality
is the class modulo three, with the quark class, the antiquark class and the neutral triangle. The lock splits along the
two primes of the figure: its twofold half is the third-integer lattice of charge, and its threefold half is the fact
that a third never terminates in base ten. Gravitation sits on the mirror's fixed stratum, with its one sign from the
shortfall at the subdominant, and the frame constant 4/3 is the simplex constant of three dimensions [Gravity, §6, p. 14; §21.8, p. 64]. The
octave is a completed limit: each rung is the ceiling of the octave below and the floor of the next, the approach one
unit short at every depth and the completion a carry. Taken together these say one thing. All matter satisfies the
lock; no unifying gauge group is claimed, since the forces sit at distinct places of one figure rather than in one
group; and there is no monopole. The position is refuted by a monopole, by a color-neutral state of non-integer
charge, or by a fourth light generation, and the registry's conditions refute its numbers.

**The nine classes without a charted reading carry structural reasons, and one of them carries the cubic invariants of
SU(3).** Of the twelve classes of §12, three have a charted reading: the figure's own form, the class of rest, force and
spine, and the alternating class of the color flows. The remaining nine carry their addresses, and each has a structural
reason. Each color flow is a permutation of three colors. The mirror inverts that permutation, and
the inversions conjugate it or relabel it. So every alternating and every blocked form keeps the product of mirror and
inversion, and that product is broken only by the mixed forms whose contained flow is odd. A form fixed by an inversion
is always fixed by the mirror. The group that a class generates together with the figure's own ordering is a class
invariant. The cyclic pairings generate Z₃ × S₃ with triality at its center, the harmonic class S₃ × S₃, and the class
of rest the pyritohedral group of a cube. Two of the open classes generate the projective group over the field of five,
and they are exactly the other forms on the figure's own projective line. The class of the two cyclic pairings is the
one whose color flows reach the three-octet sector. Its mirror-even combination is the symmetric cubic invariant of
SU(3), and its mirror-odd combination is the structure constants, the coupling of three gluons. Charge conjugation of
color acts on the flows exactly as the figure's mirror does, so this class carries both signs of the three-octet sector
and selects neither. A prohibition drawn from it would need a choice the figure does not make. Whether a mixed class
matches a known tetraquark needs a spin label, a spatial parity and a flavour label, none of which the figure supplies.
Each open class may or may not be physics in terms of our current understanding, and the method's fourth step reads the
absence of a charted reading as a prediction only once the figure is shown closed.

**The medium gives three dimensions, concealment and the linear face of gravitation from one premise, and it does not
give handedness.** The origin record identifies the count's two values with a medium of two components. On that one
premise the following hold, each exact for the model. A medium of two components carries isotropic
motion in at most three dimensions, since there are exactly three anticommuting two-by-two matrices, and an irreducible
spinor exists only in three. A lattice needs p − 1 dimensions to carry a p-fold turn, so the medium's crystal holds only the primes 2 and 3, the primes of the figure's complete rings. Every prime from 5 upward enters the arithmetic and not the geometry. Reciprocity between every pair of observers holds exactly when clocks run at the Lorentz factor, and that
factor is the medium's own interference visibility, so the Lorentz group is the condition of exact concealment and not an
added principle. The time-symmetric step's trace obeys a wave equation on the eight corners of the cube, stable at one step per tick. With matter as its source it gives the inverse square by flux conservation and a front at light's speed with light's dispersion at every order. Its static potential is the Green's function of the body-centerd cubic lattice, whose value at the source is half of Watson's integral [Watson 1939]. The static time word of the metric is linear in that potential, so the record's multiplicative rate line is the count's own compounding. With the record's two words every static, stationary and radiative field is an exact solution of Einstein's equations [Gravity].

Handedness is where the premise stops. No finite-range unitary medium on the cubic lattice is handed, with any number of components. The step and its inverse are Laurent polynomials, and the first K-group of that ring is generated by the monomial units [Bass, Heller and Swan 1964]. Every finite-range step is therefore deformable to a diagonal monomial, and its three-dimensional winding vanishes. This is the lattice doubling of Nielsen and Ninomiya in the medium's own terms
[Nielsen and Ninomiya 1981]. The routes known to the literature are a non-local step, a flavour sector obtained by a
covering of the Brillouin zone [Bakircioglu, Arnault and Arrighi 2025], and the low-energy block of a slow drive. The
route native to the register is a lossy one. The origin record's third operation rounds the count at a depth, in time,
and that rounding does not make the medium handed. A rounding of the address, one bit of each coordinate, forgets the label of the four copies into which the corner stencil divides the lattice, and it annihilates exactly the doublers. One committed bit leaves a pair of cones of opposite handedness, the four-component object three dimensions require, whose two chiral halves are the two residual copies. Two bits leave a single handed cone. The mass of the pair is the turn whose sense alternates with the copy label, while the uniform turn opens no gap in three dimensions. Whether the third
operation rounds the address as well as the count is an identification the origin record has not made. If it is made,
cosmic rays bound the rounding's rate, the committed bit count decides whether a species is Dirac matter or a single
handed cone, and a coupling that distinguishes the copies is a chiral coupling. Each of these may or may not be physics
in terms of our current understanding.

**The register's two integers are selected by closure depths that the figure does not yet supply.** A prime closes at
period d in base b exactly when it divides the cyclotomic value Φ_d(b) and no earlier one. The primes of decimal period
eight are the factors of 10⁴ + 1, which are 73 and 137 and no other; the prime of binary period fifteen is Φ₁₅(2) = 151
and it is unique. The fine-structure record and the gravity record therefore select two depths, 8 in base ten and 15 in
base two, and within the decimal depth a choice between 73 and 137. A property of the line decides that choice. A reptend read backwards that is the same line, the direction forgotten as the line theorem forgets it, is realized among all primes below 20000 by exactly four, at periods 1, 2, 4 and 8. Of those, 137's is the only one whose line leaves the figure's neutral axis: it is the triangle 0, 7, 2 walked once in each direction with a rest before each pass. The property
selects 137 within its depth, in agreement with the measured value, and it is silent on 151. What the figure does not
yet produce are the depths themselves, which the records name the octave and the joint closure of the motor 3 and the
lift 5; the figure's own closures are at depths 3 and 6. That the fine-structure constant lives in the figure's reading
base, ten, and the gravitational coupling in its doubling base, two, the two faces of the register, may or may not be
physics in terms of our current understanding.

**The case that the figure can do it rests on six facts.** The first is completeness of formal content in one line.
Section 20 asks for any other diagram whose states, dynamics, symmetry, conservation and generating operation form a
single closed line, and each standard candidate lacks part of that content. The second is that the figure selects its
own arithmetic: its line properties, stated without numbers, are realized in base ten by the nine-point figure alone,
and in no smaller base by any figure of more than two points. The third is range. Without change, the same figure carries the line theorem of §17 and an intonated layout in which seven of its nine points fall on degrees of the just major scale or on the harmonic seventh (§16). It also carries a grading that yields the minimal neutral combinations of the color sector with no gauge group supplied (§18). The fourth is that its symmetry group is exactly the automorphism group of the root
system of SU(3), computed and not assigned. The fifth is that the seven-dimensional cross product, the only one besides
the three-dimensional, can be written on the residues modulo seven that the figure's period walks, and its symmetry
group contains the color group. The sixth is precedent. Kepler published his third law in 1619 in an account of the
harmonies of the planets, after his polyhedral model of the planetary distances had proved inexact [Kepler 1619]. The
search for a harmonic order succeeded where the model did not.

**The case against it has nine parts.**

- *Provenance.* The figure is documented only from 1916. The ancient figures named in the second criterion were held by
  their peoples for millennia, and no earlier document known to the author contains the enneagram (§25).
- *Base.* The figure's nine is the modulus of digit sums in base ten, and a critic will say that its structure follows
  from a choice of base. Two answers stand beside the objection. The register theorem shows that base ten is the least
  base carrying both the digit-sum mechanism and the full period of one seventh; below eighty only 10, 19 and 73 do
  [Origin, I §2, p. 7]. And the line's own properties select base ten as the least base in which such a figure can be
  drawn. The objection keeps its force where the figure is read as a record of counting rather than as a line.
- *Selection.* Small numbers recur, and nine can be found wherever it is sought. The four choices that precede the
  derivation of §18 show where selection enters, and the units of the ring carry a second threefold structure, the Midy
  pairs, beside the grading §18 uses. A result found at a favourite number is held here to more
  scrutiny, not less, and every such result is reported with its census.
- *Labelling.* The octonion correspondence uses the standard cyclic table, and its color reading is taken from the
  received construction. The figure's mirror yields no prohibition on handedness, and the figure does not
  establish the reading of its inversions as parity or CP.
- *Magnitude.* The strongest form of the claim, a figure that yields the magnitudes of physics by arithmetic alone, is
  excluded by theorem. What remains is the ratio form together with one crossing, the identification of the theory's
  least count with an object in the world, which the corpus holds as a route and has not made.
- *Content.* A figure that returns to each observer what that observer brings forbids nothing. The description of the
  enneagram as a reflector of its readers, made elsewhere in this corpus, is in tension with the demand for a symbol
  that contains the means of unpacking.
- *Dynamics.* The figure carries no field equation of its own. Its medium carries the linear face of gravitation and,
  with the charge shared between two cell types, a dynamical electromagnetism. At one loop that medium gives the
  Schwinger term and a lattice-scale correction that the electron's measured moment excludes. Under the record's time
  rounding the count's clock runs at exactly three halves of its free rate. When light is rounded by the same rule, the
  rounded phases of the vacuum and of a three-body state two thirds of a turn above it coincide on a surface of the
  zone. Treated as a degeneracy, that surface is a channel of the rounded vacuum, with a width the mass does not change.
  Under the same rounding the count at rest is a singular point of its own dispersion, and the moment has no definite
  value there. With light's time counted in the count's units and no erasure of light, the moment is 2.08 times the
  Schwinger term, for every mass. The received field equations are adopted where the gravity record builds its
  constructions, and a rule for the vertices of a diagram from the figure's own operations remains open. One answer
  stands beside the objection. The figure is one closed stroke, prior to dimension. A field is a multiplicity of
  elements, and a multiplicity is a state that has been counted into; so a field equation is a statement about the count
  unfolding in time, and the medium, where three dimensions and the two linear faces arise from the count, is where
  field equations belong. The objection keeps its force until a vertex rule is derived from the figure's operations.
- *Handedness.* The figure's group has no parity, no finite-range unitary medium is handed, and the one handed medium
  found is lossy and rests on an identification the origin record has not made. The weak interaction, which is the
  largest sector of the Standard Model, is the one the figure reaches least.
- *Survivorship.* The precedent comes from a search that succeeded. Harmonic and numerical searches that produced
  nothing are not remembered, and the historical record does not supply their number.

**The word unitary carries the criterion's technical sense.** A companion record calls the figure unitary in two
senses: it is one object, and its dynamics, being invertible, preserves norm [Origin, Companion §3, p. 82]. Every permutation of a finite set
is invertible and preserves norm, so the second sense holds of any finite figure. The sense that carries content is the
one this section has stated: one object that is generative, closed and restrictive. On that sense the
position is definite. Formal content is met. Restriction is met, by the registered prohibitions. Generation is met in
part, for the structures the table names and not for the couplings' depths or the dynamics. Closure is met only in the
form §23 allows any complete theory, as a hypothesis with its refutation conditions.

**The question remains open, and each of the five matters defines a line of work.** The choices that precede the
derivation of §18 can be derived, or shown to be irreducible. The depths 8 and 15 at which the couplings' primes close
can be sought in the figure's own closures. The spatial word of the metric can be derived from counting, which locates it in the address register. The asymmetric two-body field can be built on the medium's wave equation with a moving source, tested first on the one case whose answer is exact. The identification of the address's rounding can be made or
refused, and if made its three consequences are stated above for measurement. A prohibition that the received theory
lacks can be sought in the corpus's own handedness, the exchange of the two orderings, by asking which interaction, if
any, is blind to it. The count's two chiralities can be brought to one momentum, so that its mass acts locally, and the lattice correction to the magnetic moment tested again. The rest values can be derived from the figure, and with them ratios of offsets that the figure, and
not a companion record, predicts. And the correspondence can be put to a tradition that holds a ninefold account in its
own terms, which is the subject of §27. Whether the figure can do it will be settled by those steps.

## 23 · Completeness: what the figure decides, and where incompleteness lives

Section 22 asked that a unitary expression be closed, needing nothing outside itself beyond the inputs it names. The
strongest form of closure is completeness. A theory is complete when every statement in its language is either proved or
refuted in it. Since 1931 the question has been governed by Gödel's theorems. This section states what those theorems
establish and shows why they do not apply to the figure. It then locates where incompleteness lives in the theories to
which they do apply, and draws the consequence for a figure offered as the expression of a physical theory.

**Gödel's theorems limit every consistent theory that contains elementary arithmetic.** The first theorem states that a
consistent theory whose axioms can be listed by a mechanical procedure, and which contains elementary arithmetic, has a
sentence that it neither proves nor refutes [Gödel 1931]. Robinson's arithmetic Q, a finite list of axioms for the
successor, addition and multiplication of whole numbers, already contains enough. The second theorem states that such a
theory cannot prove its own consistency. Gödel announced the first result at a round table in Königsberg on 7 September
1930. On the following day, in the same city, Hilbert closed an address with the words "Wir müssen wissen, wir werden
wissen" [Zach 2005].

**The theorems ended Hilbert's program in its original form, and the subject continued.** Hilbert had sought a proof, by
finitary means, that the whole of mathematics is consistent, and the second theorem is generally taken to rule this out
[Zach 2005]. Gentzen proved the consistency of arithmetic in 1936 by admitting transfinite induction up to the ordinal
ε₀ [Gentzen 1936]. Hilbert's own response, in 1931, was a rule that infers a general statement from all of its
instances, the ω-rule, to which this section returns [Hilbert 1931].

**Completeness holds wherever a theory cannot express the whole numbers with both addition and multiplication.**
Arithmetic with addition alone is complete and decidable [Presburger 1930]. So is elementary Euclidean geometry, the
first-order theory of points, lines and circles [Tarski 1951]. The complete theory of any finite structure is decided by
inspection. Incompleteness enters only where unbounded whole numbers carry both operations.

**The liar paradox concerns truth, and Gödel's theorem concerns proof.** Tarski showed that a language strong enough for
arithmetic cannot define its own truth predicate, and he located the liar paradox in languages that contain one [Tarski
1935]. Gödel noted the analogy with the liar and replaced "true" by "provable". The sentence that asserts its own
unprovability is not paradoxical: if the theory is consistent, the sentence is true and unprovable.

**The theorems have been applied well beyond their hypotheses.** Franzén examined their applications to minds, machines
and subjects outside mathematics, and what each application assumes [Franzén 2005]. Hawking argued in 2002 that a theory
of physics may be incomplete because the theory is itself part of the universe it describes [Hawking 2002]. Deutsch,
Ekert and Lupacchini argued that a proof is a physical process, whose scope depends on the physics of the device that
performs it [Deutsch et al. 2000]. The rest of this section takes that last position seriously.

**Theorem: the theory of the figure is complete.** Consider the ring of nine residues with addition, multiplication and
the figure's permutations: the period's shift, the doubling and the reflection. Call a statement about it first-order
when it is built from these operations, equality, the logical connectives, and the quantifiers "for every residue" and
"for some residue". Every such statement is decided by a finite check, so the theory of the figure is complete and
decidable.

*Proof.* Each quantifier ranges over nine residues. A statement with q quantifiers is decided by at most nine to the power q evaluations
of its quantifier-free part, and each evaluation is a finite computation in the ring. ∎

**Gödel's hypotheses fail for the figure in two places.** The ring does not contain Robinson's arithmetic: in Q no
number's successor is zero, while in the ring nine successive steps return to zero. And nine residues cannot give
distinct codes to the infinitely many statements of the language, which Gödel's construction requires in order for a
statement to refer to itself. The completeness of the figure follows from its finiteness. The single closed line of §17
is the visible form of that finiteness, and the finiteness carries the result.

**Completeness belongs to every finite structure.** What it secures for the figure is that every statement about the
figure is decided. What distinguishes the figure from other finite objects is the content established in Part I, and
completeness guarantees that this content holds no undecidable statement.

**The general theory of such figures is not complete.** Statements about every figure, for every prime and every base,
are statements about the whole numbers with both addition and multiplication, and they fall under Gödel's theorems.
Artin's conjecture, that ten is a primitive root of infinitely many primes, is a statement of this kind. It is open, and
a proof announced in October 2026 has not been formalized [OpenAI 2026]. The single figure is complete, and the general
theory of figures is not.

**Result: incompleteness lives in the step from every instance to the totality.** Let T be a consistent theory to which
Gödel's first theorem applies, and let G be its Gödel sentence. G states that for every number n, n is not the code of a
proof of G in T. Each instance, that a particular n is not such a code, is a bounded statement, settled by a finite
computation. Robinson's Q proves every true bounded statement and refutes every false one [Smith 2013]. Since T is
consistent, every instance is true, so T proves every instance of G and does not prove G itself. The property is called
ω-incompleteness.

```
T proves:            0 is not a proof of G,   1 is not a proof of G,   2 is not a proof of G,   …
T does not prove:    no number is a proof of G
```

**Admitting the step to the totality restores completeness.** The ω-rule infers "for every n, A(n)" from the instances
A(0), A(1), A(2) and so on. Arithmetic with the ω-rule in place of induction is complete: every true statement of
arithmetic is provable in it. The result is due to Schütte, and Shoenfield extended it to a computable form of the rule
[Frittaion 2021]. Gödel's theorems do not apply to it, since a proof that uses the ω-rule has infinitely many premises
and is not a finite object. Incompleteness therefore lives in exactly one step: from every finite stage, each of which
is decided, to the totality of the stages, which no finite proof reaches.

**The corpus states the same structure for numerical readings.** Unity read to n decimal places is

```
0.99…9   (n nines)   =   1 − 10⁻ⁿ
```

and it falls short of one by exactly one unit of its last place at every depth [Units, §10, p. 18]. Each reading is complete as a
reading, and one is reached by no reading. The step from every reading to one has the same form as the step from every
instance to the totality.

**Avicenna from within: what can be counted is complete, and one totality lies outside every count.** Avicenna's Proof
of the Truthful divides what exists into the contingent, which needs a cause outside itself, and the necessary, which
exists by its own nature [Avicenna]. The totality of contingent things must have a cause that is not contingent, since a
contingent cause would belong to the totality. A necessary existent therefore stands outside it. The proof proceeds from
all the members to the totality, and from the totality to a source beyond it.
