Interpretation: the results above describe the same structure from inside. Every member, every bounded statement, is
decided within the theory. The one kind of statement that cannot be decided from inside concerns the totality: the
theory as a whole over all of its stages, with its consistency as the leading case. On this reading Gödel's theorem
identifies one place, the step to the totality, and everything short of that step is decided. Hilbert's ω-rule admits
the totality as given, and with it the theory is complete.

**Continuity lives in the same step.** Three facts fix where it lives, each verified by computation
(`test_continuity_lives_in_the_step_to_the_totality`). Every finite description is countable: base-k numeration is an
exact bijection from the descriptions of length n onto the integers below kⁿ. Cantor's diagonal escapes every enumeration,
and it does so for a list of rationals given by their binary digits as readily as for a list of reals. And unity read to
depth n falls short of one by exactly one unit of its last place in every base. So everything that is described, computed
or measured is countable, and the excess of the continuum exists only as the completed totality. Continuity, like
incompleteness, lives in the step from every finite stage to the whole. The open questions of Navier–Stokes and
Yang–Mills sit in that step, which is where the published records place them. The account forbids two things: a bounded
system that distinguishes more states than the published bounds allow, and a measurement record that is not finite.

**The one external unit of §22 is a magnitude, not a sentence.** Gödel's unprovable statements are sentences, and the
figure has no undecided sentence. What the figure lacks is one magnitude: a theory takes exactly one measured quantity
beyond its dimensionless content [Units, §2, p. 3], and no dimensionless construction yields a dimensionful magnitude. The figure
is complete in its sentences and short by exactly one in its magnitudes.

**Hypothesis: every statement a physical system can perform is decided.** A performed statement is bounded by the
operations available to the system that performs it. By the completeness of Q for bounded statements, every such
statement is proved if it is true and refuted if it is false. The existence of a written statement settles that its
physical substrate exists; whether the statement is true is settled by performing it.

**The hypothesis rests on a registered measurement.** With a positive cosmological constant the entropy accessible to
any observer is bounded by 3π/Λ in Planck units, which supports a description with finitely many degrees of freedom
[Bousso 2000]. An observer in such a universe can recover and process only a finite amount of information [Krauss and
Starkman 2000], at most about 10¹²⁰ bits [Krauss and Starkman 2004]. The companion record on the vacuum commits to an
equation of state w = −1 exactly [Vacuum, §7, p. 5]. A confirmed evolution of w refutes that record and removes the clearest
ground for the hypothesis.

**The hypothesis forbids a physically realized undecidable observable.** The one place where physics has met
undecidability is the spectral gap of a quantum lattice. Cubitt, Pérez-García and Wolf proved that whether a
translation-invariant two-dimensional lattice is gapped is undecidable in the limit of infinite size [Cubitt et al.
2015]. The gap of every finite lattice is computable. The undecidability lives in the infinite limit, the idealization
the hypothesis excludes.

**An idealization completes what no performance completes.** It is exact about itself and distorted about the world, and
is thus false. The completed count adds a totality that no performance reaches; the continuum adds the infinitely fine;
the thermodynamic limit adds an infinite lattice. The falsity sits in what the idealization adds. Where a question does
not touch the addition, the falsity lies below the resolution of the question, and the idealization serves as a tool.
Where the question is the addition, the falsity is the result.

Incompleteness lies entirely in the added step to the totality. The field-theoretic estimate of the vacuum energy sums
modes that a register with a deepest cell does not contain, and it exceeds the inferred value by about 120 orders of
magnitude [Vacuum, §1, p. 1]. Cartwright argued that the fundamental laws of physics describe idealized objects in models and are
false of real systems [Cartwright 1983]. The position taken here agrees with hers and locates the falsity.

**The liar paradox has received three standard treatments.** Kripke's theory of truth leaves the liar without a truth
value [Kripke 1975]. Priest's logic of paradox gives it both values [Priest 1979]. The revision theory of Herzberger and
Gupta revises its value step by step, and the value alternates between true and false without settling [Herzberger 1982;
Gupta 1982; Gupta and Belnap 1993]. Spencer-Brown treated a form that re-enters itself as taking an imaginary value, an
oscillation in time, and Kauffman and Varela developed the dynamics of such forms [Spencer-Brown 1969; Kauffman and
Varela 1980].

**Theorem: on a ring of odd order, negation has exactly one fixed point.** Read negation as the figure's reflection, d ↦
−d modulo n. A residue equal to its own negation exists only at 0 when n is odd, and at 0 and n/2 when n is even. On the
ring of nine the reflection exchanges 3 and 6 and fixes 9, which is 0. Neither modulo 7 nor modulo 9 does −1 have a
square root.

Interpretation: the triangle {3, 6, 9}, with the reflection as negation, carries the negation of a three-valued logic,
with two values exchanged and one fixed. The liar, a statement equal to its own negation, can take only the fixed value.
Revised step by step, it alternates between 3 and 6, which is the oscillation of the revision theory. The value at which
it is consistent is the residue that is both 9 and 0, so Kripke's "neither" and Priest's "both" fall on one point of the
ring. An imaginary value would require a square root of −1, and the figure's rings have none. Within the figure the liar
exists only as the alternation and its fixed point.

**Theorem: a chain of negations around the ring of nine breaks at the antipode.** Label each point of a ring with one of
two values, and require each point to differ from its neighbour. On a ring of odd length the requirement fails at an odd
number of bonds, so at least once; on a ring of even length it can hold at every bond. On the ring of nine every
labelling symmetric under the reflection gives 4 and 5 the same value, so the requirement fails on the bond between
them, the one bond the reflection fixes. Of the eighteen labellings with a single failure, exactly two are symmetric,
and both fail there. A labelling that the reflection reverses cannot assign a value to
9, the one point equal to its own image.

In condensed-matter physics a system whose couplings cannot all be satisfied is called frustrated [Toulouse 1977].
Interpretation: the reflection's axis holds both parts of the paradox. The liar's consistent value lies at the root, and
the break that a chain of negations cannot avoid lies at the antipode. The two mirror-reversed labellings of §22's third example
disagree exactly on the pair beside that bond, and exchanging 4 and 5 turns one into the other.

**Result: in a quantum ring of odd length, the ground state spreads the frustration evenly, and its standing waves
center it on the antipodal pair.** The spin-½ antiferromagnet on a ring is the quantum form of a chain of negations,
each moment preferring its neighbour opposite. For every odd ring the ground state is fourfold: two doublets of total
spin one half, with opposite momenta [Bärwinkel et al. 2003]. Exact diagonalization of the rings of three to eleven
spins, carried out for this section, gives three further facts.

In the ground states of definite momentum, which carry a definite handedness, every bond has the same correlation. In
the standing wave that the reflection leaves unchanged, the weakest bond is the antipodal bond and the unpaired spin is
concentrated at the root. In the standing wave that the reflection reverses, the antipodal bond is the strongest, and
the weakest bonds flank it. A change at the root selects between the two: weakening the root's two bonds by a tenth
makes the first the ground state, and strengthening them makes the second.

Interpretation: the frustration of an odd ring is present in its ground state, distributed evenly and located nowhere,
as long as no point of the ring is distinguished. A distinction at the root resolves it into a standing wave centerd on
4 and 5, either as the break between them or as the strongest bond between them. Part I found the same pair as the two
points of the intonated layout that never reach a just interval, at thirteen and fourteen ninths, with mean 3/2 (§16).
The figure supplies the two places on the axis, and quantum mechanics supplies the wave that organizes itself around
them. Local probes of bond correlation in odd molecular rings, such as the heptanuclear vanadyl ring with its two nearly
degenerate doublets of spin one half [Newton et al. 2016], can read which standing wave a given ring adopts.

**Physical systems perform the liar in three ways.**

- *As a clock.* An inverter wired to its own input settles at its switching voltage, between the two logic levels. That
  self-biased inverter, driving a quartz crystal, forms the Pierce oscillator used in most digital clocks. A ring of an
  odd number N of inverters oscillates with a period of 2N gate delays. A ring of an even number holds one of two
  states, which is how a memory cell stores a bit. Each bit of a binary counter changes at every carry into it, so the
  lowest bit repeats the liar once per count.
- *As frustration.* A ring of an odd number of antiferromagnetically coupled spins is frustrated. The first such
  molecular ring, eight chromium ions and one nickel ion, nine in all, was reported in 2004 and named the magnetic
  Möbius strip [Cador et al. 2004]. In a triangle of three spin-½ copper ions the lowest states form two doublets of
  opposite chirality, and an electric field can drive the molecule between them [Trif et al. 2008].
- *As a phase.* A spin-½ particle rotated through 2π returns with its state multiplied by −1, and rotated through 4π
  returns to itself. Neutron interferometry measured this sign in 1975 [Rauch et al. 1975; Werner et al. 1975]. No
  measurement on one beam detects it, because every observable is quadratic in the state; interference between a rotated
  and an unrotated beam does.

Interpretation: in each case a statement that is its own negation becomes a definite structure: a frequency, a defect
that moves around a ring, or a phase visible only in interference. In a physical ring the break sits where the couplings
are weakest, so the figure's placement of it at the antipode describes the case of equal couplings with the reflection
kept.

**This section establishes four results, and four questions remain open.** The theory of the figure is complete.
Incompleteness, where it holds, lives in the step from every finite stage to the totality, and admitting that step
restores completeness. On the ring of nine the liar has one consistent value, at the root, and one unavoidable break, at
the antipode. Physical systems perform the liar as a frequency, a moving defect or a phase. The first open question is
the hypothesis that physical arithmetic is bounded, which rests on the registered value w = −1. The second is whether
the one external reference of §22 and the one totality of this section are the same. The third is a test of the
antipodal reading in a physical ring of nine. The fourth is whether the figure underlies every structure built of lines,
which is the strongest form the claim could take.

## 24 · The special case of the atomic mass unit: a hypothesis

The one magnitude that §22 leaves open and §23 locates has a candidate in the corpus, and this section states the
candidate as a hypothesis with what establishes and what refutes it. The candidate is the atomic mass unit, and the
basic statement is that it satisfies three conditions at once. It is native: a twelfth of the mass of carbon-12 is the
structure's own object, and masses in that unit are ratios to it. It carries a dimension: the unit is a mass. And its
value has a dimensionless origin: the corpus places the archetypal nucleon at 1.008 in that unit from the dimensionless
register. Nativity, dimensionality and dimensionless origin together are what the hypothesis below claims no other
object has.

**The theorem on scale is true, and the premise that the one magnitude is borrowed does not hold here.** A pure number
carries no unit, and every measured length, mass or time is a comparison with a reference. That is what measurement is,
so the theorem does not move. What does not follow is that the reference lies outside the theory. In a discrete theory
the world carries its own standard, the least count. The theory then determines every ratio and every count, one
measurement fixes the dictionary into conventional units, and the ratios themselves need no measurement. The metre and
the kilogram are the arbitrary objects; the count is not.

**Hypothesis: the atomic mass unit is the realized dimensionally complete unit.** Masses in atomic mass units are ratios
to a twelfth of the mass of carbon-12, so a rest value in those units is dimensionless and lies inside what the theorem
permits, while the unit itself is a mass. The corpus places the archetypal nucleon at 1.008 in those units and anchors it
to the dimensionless register [Proton and neutron, §6, p. 6]. The hypothesis is that this unit is the one object in the world that
is at once a count of the structure and a magnitude: the means by which the dimensioned and the dimensionless coexist.
In the terms of Avicenna from within, it is the one dimensioned unit that shows up on the inside.
The two do coexist, and their coexistence demands a non-abstract means by which both conditions hold at once; the
hypothesis names that means. On it the theorem on scale keeps its general truth and admits exactly one instance in which
the calibration is not borrowed but derived, in the theory's own units. The form is Hilbert's proposal at the level of
units: completeness restored by admitting one object as given, except that here the object is realized and not
postulated. It is also the converse of Gödel's situation: the one magnitude that no sentence of the theory decides is
decided by the world's own unit.

**What establishes the hypothesis, and what refutes it.** The step that establishes it is a ruler-free functional whose
unique minimum is the anchor, so that the value at 1.008 is determined and not matched; the corpus holds the route and has not
made the step. It is refuted in three ways. A measured nucleon rest value, in atomic mass units, away from the placed value beyond
the registered conditions refutes it [Proton and neutron, §14, p. 16; Schedule, §11, p. 11]. So does a functional with no minimum at the anchor.
So does a second independent object that fills the same role, since the unit the hypothesis names must be unique. Until the step is made,
the one magnitude remains the figure's shortfall, and the hypothesis is the stated form of the crossing.

**If the hypothesis holds, it bears on how what has no dimension and no time comes into being.** The dimensionless
register holds its ratios without magnitude and without epoch, as potentials: a value is one thing and enters time only
when read, as §22's reading of 3/7 shows. The unit named here would be the point at which such a potential acquires a
magnitude, and with it a place and an epoch, without any quantity being borrowed from outside. That is a reading, held as
one; its content is the hypothesis above, and it stands or falls with it.

## 25 · The resurrection of a symbol: the enneagram's history and its readings

Sections 25 to 27 contain no derivations and no claims of result, and are provided only to establish context and
provenance.

**The earliest documented presentation of the figure is in P. D. Ouspensky's account of George Gurdjieff's teaching in
Russia, published after Ouspensky's death [Ouspensky 1949, chapter XIV].** Ouspensky places it in the second half of
1916, between talks he dates to the summer and to the end of that year. Gurdjieff presented a circle divided into nine
parts, with lines joining the points in a certain order, as the symbol that unites the law of seven, his name for the
structure of the octave, with the law of three.

The construction Ouspensky records is arithmetic. The points of the six-point path are taken from the sevenths written
as decimals, 1/7 = 0.142857 and its multiples, and joined in the order of the period. An equilateral triangle joins the
three points the period never reaches, and its apex is the point where the cycle begins and ends. Gurdjieff therefore
stated the figure's connection to one seventh. He did not prove what fixes its parts, and the account does not ask why
the path and the triangle divide the nine points as they do.

**The construction comes with an extended explanation in the teaching's own terms.** The record states that the decimal system is constructed on the same laws as the octave [Ouspensky 1949, ch. XIV, p. 204]. It states also that the symbolism of numbers cannot be understood without a clear conception of how octaves are expressed in the decimal system [Ouspensky 1949, ch. XIV, p. 201]. Earlier, the teaching sets the discontinuity of vibrations, which it attributes to ancient knowledge, against the view usual in the West that vibrations are continuous [Ouspensky 1949, ch. VII, p. 91]. In *Beelzebub's Tales* the narrator holds that no apparatus then existing in Europe could count vibrations exactly, and calls the acoustic siren a "childish bauble" [Gurdjieff 1950, ch. XLI]. The explanation is extended and, by evident intention, indirect. Ouspensky judged it unfinished, with hints at a continuation [Ouspensky 1949, ch. XVIII, p. 268].

Gurdjieff described the figure as a universal symbol in which all knowledge could be included, and as a schematic
diagram of perpetual motion whose knowledge had until then been kept secret. He insisted that it be understood in
motion: "A motionless enneagram is a dead symbol; the living symbol is in motion." His pupils performed it as movement,
standing on the numbered points of a figure drawn on the floor and moving in the order of the period. A further drawing,
made under his direction in Constantinople in 1920, set the four beasts of the Apocalypse and a dove inside the figure.

Ouspensky extended the figure in his own work. He related it to Gurdjieff's diagram of three interacting octaves in the
human organism [Ouspensky 1949, chapter XVIII]. He also noted that its seven points could represent the seven planets
of the ancient world, so that the figure could be read as an astronomical symbol.

**The figure's source before 1916 is not established.** Gurdjieff named none: he described the teaching as independent
of other lines and said the symbol could not be found in the literature of occultism. From the late 1960s the figure
was presented as the device of a Sufi school, in accounts of Oscar Ichazo's teaching [Lilly and Hart 1975] and later in
the opening of Helen Palmer's book [Palmer 1988]. The author knows of no Sufi text earlier than 1916 that contains the
figure, and a citation that corrects this is asked for.

Earlier diagrams have also been proposed as precursors. One of those named, the frontispiece of Athanasius Kircher's
*Arithmologia* of 1665, sets the nine orders of angels on three interlaced triangles [Kircher 1665]. It contains the
enneagram's triangle and not its six-point path, and the author knows of no earlier figure that contains the path.

**In the 1970s the figure acquired a second use, as a typology of character.** Oscar Ichazo, who founded the Arica
School in Chile, placed nine fixations of the ego on the nine points of the figure [Ichazo 1982]. Claudio Naranjo, a
participant in the training Ichazo gave to Americans in Arica at the start of the 1970s, developed the nine points into
a psychology of character types [Naranjo 1990, 1994]. Through Naranjo the typology reached Robert Ochs, a Jesuit who
taught it at the Jesuit School of Theology in Chicago in 1971–72 [Jesuits Midwest 2018; International Enneagram
Association 2018]. The books of Don Richard Riso, Helen Palmer, and Richard Rohr and Andreas Ebert carried it to a
general readership [Riso 1987; Palmer 1988; Rohr and Ebert 1990].

In 1992 the Arica Institute's suit against Palmer and her publisher reached the Court of Appeals for the Second
Circuit. The court held that the sequence of the fixations was not protected by copyright and that Palmer's use was
fair. The opinion records that Ichazo did not originate the nine-pointed figure [Arica Institute v. Palmer 1992]. The typology
has since been studied as a psychological instrument. A systematic review of 104 independent samples found the evidence
for its reliability and validity mixed, and no study it reviewed derived nine types by clustering [Hook et al. 2021].

**Several readings of the figure are now in circulation.** Gurdjieff read it as a diagram of process, uniting the laws
of seven and three, and as a figure of perpetual motion. Ouspensky added a reading in terms of three octaves and an
astronomical one. Ichazo and Naranjo read it as a map of character, and Rohr and Ebert as a tool of Christian
contemplation. Part I of this volume reads it as an object of modular arithmetic. The readings differ in what they
take the nine points to be: steps of a process, planets, types of person, or residues.

**The resurrection the title names is a return to the figure in motion.** Gurdjieff's condition was that the figure be
read in motion. The typology reads the nine points as positions, each the station of a character, and uses the figure
as a map at rest. Part I reads every element of the figure as an operation. The six-point line is the orbit of the
period's shift and, in the other order, the orbit of multiplication by two modulo nine (§4). The reflection that pairs
each point with its nine-complement is the half-period negation of Midy's theorem (§5). The involution that exchanges the
two orders is the figure's generating operation (§4). Along the doubling orbit the two charged classes of the grading
alternate at every step (§18), and the whole figure, drawn in one stroke, can be executed in 8668 ways (§17).

In each case the motion is exact and can be checked. In this sense, which is Gurdjieff's, the figure is again read in
motion, and its motions are theorems.

Gurdjieff's two laws have definite counterparts in mathematics and physics. The law of seven corresponds to the period
of one seventh, which a companion record proposes as a mathematical constant [The Seventh]. It corresponds also to the
arithmetic of bounded harmonic systems that the corpus develops from that period [Origin]. A cross product of vectors exists only in three and
in seven dimensions [Hurwitz 1898; Massey 1983], and the seven-dimensional one can be written on the residues modulo
seven (§22). The law of three corresponds to the figure's threefold grading, which §18 identifies with triality, the
threefold grading of color. In the received theory, electric charge modulo one is fixed by triality for every known
particle: states of triality zero carry whole charges, and quarks carry thirds [Baez and Huerta 2010]. Where the figure
came from remains open.

## 26 · Nine in the ancient cosmologies: whole-number pictures of the world

Several of the oldest recorded cosmologies describe the order of the world in whole numbers, and some give the number
nine an explicit office: nine gods, nine worlds, nine kingdoms. The comparative study of number symbolism catalogues
these cases beside the more numerous sevens, twelves and fours [Schimmel 1993]. The cases cited here are those in which
the nine is stated in the source itself.

The Pyramid Texts of the Fifth and Sixth Dynasties name the Great Ennead of Heliopolis [Allen 2005]. It is the creator
Atum, his children Shu and Tefnut, their children Geb and Nut, and their children Osiris, Isis, Seth and Nephthys: one
origin and four pairs. The Völuspá opens with a seeress who remembers nine worlds [Larrington 2014, stanza 2]. Among the
Kogui of the Sierra Nevada de Santa Marta, the people's own published account speaks of nine Fathers and Mothers of
order, nine classes of land, and nine layers of the earth [Organización Gonawindúa Tayrona 2018, pp. 45, 49, 126]. An
outside description, Reichel-Dolmatoff's, has the Mother create the universe by thinking a ninefold structure: four
worlds above, four below, and the world of people between them [Reichel-Dolmatoff 1987].

Frank Waters's account of Hopi tradition, compiled with Oswald White Bear Fredericks from the testimony of elders,
describes nine universal kingdoms. One belongs to the creator Taiowa, one to his nephew Sótuknang, and seven to the
life to come [Waters 1963]. The account's standing as a record of Hopi tradition is disputed [Geertz 1994]. In the
Bahá'í Faith, a letter written on behalf of Shoghi Effendi in 1939 gives three reasons for the regard in which nine is
held [Hornby 1994]. They are the nine religions of which history has definite knowledge, perfection as the highest
single number, and the abjad value of the word Bahá.

Nine has more than one source in ordinary experience. It is the last digit before a decimal count returns to one, it is
three times three, and it is the customary count of the months of human gestation. Any of these can produce a ninefold
picture of the world without a common origin. A survey that counts the cases of a number, and not the cases without
it, will find whichever number it sets out to find. The cases above are cited for the explicitness of their nine, not
as a sample.

What these cases establish is accordingly narrow. Several traditions chose a whole number to carry the order of the
world, chose nine for it, and kept that structure over long periods. Whether they share a source remains open. Whether
the arithmetic of Part I bears on why they chose it is part of the question stated in §22.

The Sufi tradition, often named in connection with the figure, belongs to the preceding section. Its association with the
enneagram rests on a claim about the figure's provenance, not on a ninefold cosmology.

---

## 27 · A living tradition: the Sierra Nevada de Santa Marta
<!-- lab status: as drafted 7 October; awaiting José's clearance before any deposit. -->

The Sierra Nevada de Santa Marta, an isolated mountain massif on the Caribbean coast of Colombia, is home to four
peoples: the Kogui, the Arhuaco, the Wiwa and the Kankuamo. In 2022 UNESCO inscribed their shared ancestral system of
knowledge on its Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity [UNESCO 2022].

**The tradition's continuity reaches back before any written record of the region.** When the Spanish arrived, the
Tairona held the northern slopes of the Sierra, and they were finally defeated in 1600. The archaeological record
places the Tairona's ancestors in the region about eighteen hundred years ago, and dates the oldest dwellings at
Teyuna, the site known as Ciudad Perdida, to around 650 CE [ICANH 2019]. The conventional account, that of
anthropology, describes the Kogui as descendants of the Tairona [Reichel-Dolmatoff 1982]. The Kogui's own account,
which they are now transmitting to readers outside the tradition and much of which is new to the outside record,
describes instead an unbroken continuity of their own people. In it the mamas, in charge since long before the Spanish
arrived, still manage the territory as one unit under the principles of the Law of Origin. The Kogui, with the Arhuaco,
the Wiwa and the Kankuamo, have defended their identity and their territory without a break since before that
arrival. The account examines the view of some anthropologists and historians that the peoples of the Sierra are a
product of the colonial period. It finds no support for that view in the documents of 1550 to 1810, and rejects it [Organización
Gonawindúa Tayrona 2018, p. 206].

**The tradition is held by trained keepers.** The Kogui's own account names the places
set aside for the formation of new mamas and authorities, where music, dance, history and the instruments are taught
[Organización Gonawindúa Tayrona 2018, p. 103]. Reichel-Dolmatoff described the mamas' apprenticeship, from early
childhood, as lasting about eighteen years, in two periods of nine [Reichel-Dolmatoff 1976]. The tradition's cosmology
is ninefold in its own terms, as §26 records: its own published account speaks of nine Fathers and Mothers of order,
nine classes of land and nine layers of the earth [Organización Gonawindúa Tayrona 2018, pp. 45, 49, 126]. Whether the ninefold account is as old as the tradition itself is a question the
record does not settle.

**The keepers have chosen to address those outside the tradition.** In 1990 the mamas chose to address the outside world, with
a warning about the damage being done to the earth, through a film made with them and an account of its making [Ereira
1990a, 1990b]. In 2018 the
Organización Gonawindúa Tayrona published its own account of the tradition [Organización Gonawindúa Tayrona 2018].

**Together these facts define an avenue of inquiry that, to the author's knowledge, exists in no other place.** Each
fact is found elsewhere on its own. Other traditions are living, other cosmologies are ninefold, and other peoples have
spoken to the modern world. In the Sierra Nevada the three coincide: a tradition whose continuity reaches back before
the written record, a cosmology whose order is stated as nine in its own terms, and keepers who have chosen to speak to
those outside it.

**The coincidence matters for the fifth matter of §22.** That matter asks for an account of the recurrence of nine that
can be tested against a tradition's own statements. Elsewhere such a test can only be made against texts, read at a
distance and through translation, which is where comparative readings go astray (§26). In the Sierra Nevada the
statements can be asked for, and an account can be put to the people who hold the tradition, on their terms.

**An inquiry of this kind has conditions.** It asks rather than supplies: the tradition's own account of its nine comes
first, and resemblances found by the inquirer are not evidence. What the holders of the tradition conclude is theirs to
state. Whether such an inquiry takes place, and in what form, is for them to decide.

The question of §22 is therefore not only a question for mathematics and physics. One of its parts can be put to a
tradition that still holds a ninefold account of the world.

---

## 28 · Index of theorems and their formulations

Each theorem of the record is stated here once, in one formulation, with the section or
battery that establishes it. The first three groups are theorems about the figure as a mathematical object. The fourth group collects the results for the models that §22 cites. Each is exact for the model it names and rests on the premise stated with it. Its physical reading is a separate matter, decided in the registry and not here.

**Part I: the figure as a mathematical object.**

1. *The figure* (§2). Nine points at 40° carry the hexad {1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 8}, the units of the ring of nine, and the
   triad {3, 6, 9}, its non-units, with 9 standing for 0.
2. *The cycle* (§3). For m coprime to the base B, the orbit of the powers of B modulo m is a cyclic group, and its
   length is the period of 1/m.
3. *The two orders* (§4). The positional order 1, 4, 2, 8, 5, 7 and the multiplicative order 1, 2, 4, 8, 7, 5 are two
   Hamiltonian cycles on the same six points.
4. *Midy's theorem, drawn* (§5). For a prime of even period 2h the two half-blocks sum digitwise to B − 1; on the figure
   this is the point reflection d ↔ 9 − d.
5. *The gallery of multiplication* (§6). The times-k diagram on N points has an envelope depending on k alone; doubling's
   envelope is the cardioid r = (2/3)(1 + cos φ) about (−1/3, 0), tripling's the nephroid.
6. *The ladder of best approximation* (§7). The equation 2ᵖ = 3^q has no solution, so log₂3 is irrational, and its
   convergents are the musical commas and the scale sizes, twelve among them.
7. *The rings and the scales* (§8). The transpositional stabilizer of a subset of the twelve-cycle decides what interior
   evidence can distinguish, and the diatonic set is the one with trivial stabilizer among its kind.
8. *The spectrum of the figure* (§9). The Dirichlet form on the nine-cycle is even and mirror-invariant, and its
   eigenvalues are the figure's spectrum.
9. *The cyclotomic tower* (§10). 2ᵈ − 1 is the product of the cyclotomic values Φ_e(2) over the divisors e of d; each
   depth mints its own prime, and 151 is Φ₁₅(2), born at the depth 15 = 3 × 5.
10. *The register* (§11). The construction runs in base B exactly when B ≡ 1 modulo 9 and B is a primitive root
    modulo 7; ten is the least such base.
11. *The sixty forms* (§12). There are exactly sixty Hamiltonian cycles on the hexad, read as closed six-digit words,
    and the figure's two orders are two of them.
12. *The modulus expanded* (§14). What changes when the circle has N points rather than nine; the ordering and the
    circle are different objects.
13. *The five structured forms* (§15). The four balanced forms, read as six-digit numbers, are exactly the multiples of
    999, and the convex form is the one that is a multiple of 111.
14. *The intonated layout* (§16). Ordering, which points join, and layout, where points sit, are independent, and the
    intonated layout places the points by the just ratios.
15. *The line theorem* (§17). The digit path of 1/p in base b is one closed stroke through distinct points exactly when
    b is a primitive root modulo p, and the stroke count is (p − 1)/ord_p(b).
16. *The grading is unique* (§18). The figure admits exactly one grading into three classes compatible with its
    operations: {1, 4, 7}, {2, 5, 8}, {3, 6, 9}.
17. *The orbit alternates and never lands* (§18). The doubling orbit alternates between the two charged classes and
    never reaches the neutral axis.
18. *The minimal neutrals* (§18). The smallest neutral combinations are a class with its partner and three of one
    class; their identification with mesons and baryons is stated as an identification.
19. *The complete figure, five parts* (the companion record, Theorem M2). For (7, 10) the stroke is unicursal through six points, and those points are exactly the doubling orbit modulo nine. The untouched residues are exactly the triangle, the point reflection is Midy's half-period shift, and the first operation is the map between the two Hamiltonian cycles.

**The new chapters of the definitive edition.**

20. *The twelve classes* (§12.1). The twelve symmetries sort the sixty forms into exactly twelve classes, of sizes 1,
    2, 3, 3, 3, 6, 6, 6, 6, 6, 6, 12.
21. *Each class has its own address* (§12.2). The residue pattern, the complement chords and the symmetry profile
    identify every class, and no two classes share them.
22. *The transforms* (§13.1–13.4). Up to symmetry there are twelve transforms out of the doubling form, and the first operation is the only non-trivial one that commutes with the mirror. The transforms that keep the residue classes are exactly those onto the alternating forms, and four classes contain no involutive transform.
23. *The Klein group* (§13.5). On the four interior units the mirror, the inversion and the first operation form one
    Klein four-group.
24. *The inversions* (§13.6). Of the seven involutive symmetries only the three inversions keep each residue class;
    they are conjugate; they do not commute with the class rotations; and they are the Weyl reflections of SU(3)'s root
    system, not a parity.
25. *The mixed forms* (§13.7). A mirror composed with an inversion fixes a mixed form exactly when the pairing it
    contains is even.
26. *The forms as diagrams* (§19.1–19.3). The sixty forms are the sixty orderings of six legs around one loop; three
    cross-ratios identify every class; a form winds about the hexagon's center in a definite way exactly when no leg
    carries the half-turn.
27. *The color-flow basis* (§19.4). With §18's identification, the six alternating forms are the color-flow basis.

**Part II.**

28. *The theory of the figure is complete* (§23). The ring of nine residues with its operations has a complete theory.
29. *Where incompleteness lives* (§23). A consistent theory of all instances is incomplete only in passing from every
    instance to the whole, and the ω-rule restores completeness.
30. *Negation on the ring* (§23). Negation on a ring of odd order has exactly one fixed point; a chain of negations
    around the ring of nine breaks at the antipode; the quantum ring of odd length spreads the frustration evenly.

**Results exact for the models named, with their premises.**

31. *The symmetry group* (`test_the_colour_flow_and_symmetry_of_the_classes`). The twelve maps are the automorphism group of the A₂ root system, the Weyl group of SU(3) with the central reflection. The inversions are color's Weyl reflections and the orbit's orientation reversals, and the mirror is −1 on the weights and crossing on momenta. The signature of a parity is absent.
32. *The profiles* (same). Under the mirror and an inversion the sixty forms partition as 10, 32, 6 and 12, and no form
    is fixed by an inversion alone.
33. *The line selects its arithmetic* (`test_the_line_selects_its_arithmetic`). The figure's line properties alone are realized below base 3000 by exactly four figures, on the digit rings of 2, 4, 9 and 729 elements. The nine-point figure is the smallest with more than two points and the only one in base ten, and ten is the least base carrying one.
34. *The cross-ratios split along 2 and 3* (`test_the_cross_ratios_split_along_two_and_three`). On the doubling hexagon
    every cross-ratio is 9^β · 4^α, β the flow and α the partner geometry, and the Parke–Taylor denominators at the
    sixth roots are real with sign the winding parity.
35. *The amplitude rule's relations* (`test_the_amplitude_rule_on_the_ring`). Every symmetric planar local rule obeys
    three linear relations among the twelve class values, cubic-only rules a fourth; the relations are generic while
    the collapse of sixty forms to twelve classes never occurs by chance.
36. *The lock on the figure* (`test_the_lock_rests_on_one_premise`). Q + t/3 is an integer on every figure state, from one premise of color's type together with two choices already in the record, by the ring's single threefold quotient. In the Standard Model the statement is the Z₆ center condition.
37. *The period-eight primes* (`test_the_register_to_figure_link_first_pass`). The primes of decimal period eight are exactly the factors of 10⁴ + 1 = 73 × 137. The reptend of 1/137 is the polar flip's product from the seed tone 72 and a reversal-symmetric necklace, one of three primes below 5000 with that property, because 73 ≡ 1 modulo 9.
38. *The register's selection reduces to depths* (`test_the_registers_selection_second_pass`). A prime closes at period d in base b exactly when it divides Φ_d(b) and no earlier value, and 151 is the unique prime of binary period fifteen. Among all primes below 20000 the reptend necklace equal to its own reversal occurs for exactly 3, 11, 101 and 137, each block a palindrome. Of those, 137's line is the only one off the neutral axis, the triangle 0, 7, 2 walked once each way.
39. *The figure's kinematics is physical* (`test_the_particle_reading_at_the_figures_kinematics`; premise: the dual
    coordinates). Spinors on the doubling hexagon with momenta signed by triality give six massless conserved momenta
    with invariants +3, −1, −4, the equilateral member of the common-frame backscatter family.
40. *The single color structure* (`test_the_six_quark_amplitude_has_one_colour_structure`; premises: the rules of QCD,
    massless quarks of distinct flavours, tree level). On the backscatter family, of which the figure's point is the
    equilateral member, the six-quark amplitude has the one color structure f^abc for every helicity, because the two gluon
    orderings cancel on each line, a cancellation that is gauge-invariant, by Chisholm's identity and the reflection; the zero
    holds for an odd number of lines and not an even one, as time-reversal with T² = (−1)^N requires; the QED analogue vanishes
    on the whole family.
41. *The junction operator* (`test_the_full_six_quark_amplitude_at_the_figure`). f^abc T^a T^b T^c equals (i/4)(C − C⁻¹),
    with C the cyclic permutation of the three color slots, so it is odd under exchange and invariant, annihilates the
    singlet and the decuplet, and squares to 3/16 of the projector onto the two octets; the four equal shares hold in
    covariant gauges at the equilateral point only.
42. *Concealment is the quadratic budget* (`test_the_concealment_swing`; premise: the count as a medium of two values).
    Reciprocity for every pair of observers holds exactly when clocks run at √(1 − β²); the budget is the mass shell;
    radar coordinates are then Lorentz.
43. *The anatomy of the walk* (`test_the_anatomy_of_the_medium`). Rest is a standing wave; velocity is |R|² − |L|²; the
    Lorentz factor is the visibility 2|R||L|; matter's top speed is cos θ.
44. *Three dimensions from two components* (`test_the_medium_in_three_dimensions`, `test_the_prime_structure_of_the_medium`).
    There is no fourth anticommuting two-by-two matrix; the irreducible two-component spinor exists only in three
    dimensions; a lattice needs p − 1 dimensions for a p-fold turn, so the crystal holds only 2 and 3.
45. *The doublers are phase images* (`test_the_doublers_are_phase_images`). U(K + q) = U(Rq); the corner state is the
    zero-momentum state times a checkerboard sign; two components cannot gap the doublers; four components gap them
    only through β.
46. *No finite-range unitary walk is handed* (same battery). The step and its inverse are Laurent polynomials, and the first K-group of the Laurent ring is generated by the monomial units. Every finite-range walk is therefore homotopic to a diagonal monomial, and its three-dimensional winding vanishes, for any number of components.
47. *Rounding and the doublers* (`test_what_rounding_does_to_the_doublers`, `test_the_origins_rounding_and_the_two_registers`).
    A rounding that forgets the copy label annihilates exactly the doublers and leaves a handed low-energy medium, local and non-unitary, at a loss of (|k|²/4)⁴ per tick. The rounding of the count at a depth, in time, removes only the quasienergy-π sector and leaves the medium unhanded.
48. *The address rounding bit by bit* (`test_the_address_rounding_bit_by_bit`). Rounding the address bit of one coordinate removes every cone with that momentum component at π. One bit of x or z leaves a pair of opposite handedness, one bit of y two of the same, and two or three bits the single cone. The uniform turn opens no gap in three dimensions, while the turn alternating with the residual copy label opens exactly the gap m at every cone, with E² = q² + m².
49. *The gravity face on the corner stencil* (`test_the_gravity_face_on_the_corner_stencil`; premise: matter as the
    source on the trace). The eight corners split the cubic lattice into four body-centerd copies labelled by the four massless points. The static potential is the body-centerd cubic Green's function, with G(0) = Γ(1/4)⁴/(8π³), and the flux through every cube is the source. The face is ballistic and causal, it settles onto the potential, and matter spreads equally over the copies.
50. *The two words of the metric* (`test_the_two_words_on_the_stencil`). For f = e^{2ψ} the static vacuum equation f Δf = (∇f)² equals 2f² Δψ, so it is linear in the deficit. On the lattice the product rule is exact and the vacuum residual is third order in the deficit. On the stencil's wave face light bends and is delayed once by a deficit in the count alone and twice by one in count and address alike.
51. *The shortfall of a complete cycle* (`test_the_shortfall_is_one_in_every_base`; the units record). If m is prime to
    the base b, the period of 1/m has k places and B is its block, then bᵏ − m·B = 1 in every base.

**The figure as an object: the open classes and Hilbert's Father.** Results about the figure as an object, each with its
battery. The results on the medium, electromagnetism on the pair, the one-loop moment, the count's mass and the
erasure, belong to the gravity and medium records and are not indexed here.

52. *The forms beside matter* (`test_the_forms_beside_matter`). The group a form's ordering generates with the figure's
    own is a class invariant. It is Z₆ for the figure and Z₃ × S₃ for the cyclic pairings, with triality at its center.
    It is S₃ × S₃ for the harmonic class and the pyritohedral group of a cube for the class of rest. It is PGL(2, 5) for
    two open classes and S₆ for the other six.
53. *Matter's projective line* (`test_five_and_eight_are_matters_projective_line`). Each 6-cycle of S₆ lies in exactly
    one of its six transitive S₅ subgroups, so each form has one projective line over the field of five. The figure's
    line carries the figure, the three forms of one open class and the six of another. The twelve symmetries act on it
    as Möbius maps.
54. *The cyclic pairings carry the cubic invariants* (`test_class_two_carries_the_cubic_invariants`). Of the six color
    flows only the two cyclic pairings reach the three-octet sector. Their mirror-even sum is the symmetric invariant d
    and their mirror-odd difference the structure constants f. At two colors the flows have rank five and d vanishes.
55. *The mirror is charge conjugation on color* (`test_class_two_forbids_nothing_without_a_new_choice`). Exchanging
    each pair's color indices sends a pairing to its inverse, as the mirror does; on the octets d is charge-even and f
    charge-odd; the cyclic pairings carry both signs and select neither.
56. *Every profile has a reason* (`test_every_profile_has_a_structural_reason`). Each color flow is a permutation of
    three colors. The mirror inverts it, and the inversions conjugate or relabel it. Every alternating and blocked form
    keeps the product of mirror and inversion, and the product is broken only by the mixed forms whose contained flow
    is odd.
57. *The empty cell* (`test_the_empty_cell_is_a_corollary`). A form fixed by an inversion is fixed by the mirror, on all
    sixty forms; the converse fails on one class alone.
58. *The C sectors of the mixed classes* (`test_the_tetraquark_question_needs_an_identification`). The mirror maps each
    mixed class to itself, with even and odd dimensions 3 and 3, 6 and 0, 3 and 3, 3 and 3, 6 and 6. Reading a
    tetraquark's quantum numbers from a form needs a spin label, a spatial parity and a flavour label.
59. *The fourfold point is a generalized residue* (`test_the_hexagons_fourfold_contribution_is_a_generalized_residue`).
    With the figure's kinematics pushed into four dimensions, four of the six solutions collapse onto the regular hexagon
    and carry the rank-four share of the amplitudes; the two that stay apart are the cyclic pairings and carry the
    rank-two share.
60. *The nine-level space and its inner product* (`test_hilberts_father_the_formal_content`). On the functions on the
    residues modulo nine the step and the clock obey CS = ωSC. Doubling is a unitary symmetry of the pair that keeps the
    hexad and the triangle apart. The sesquilinear forms invariant under both are the multiples of the counting form
    alone.
61. *The relation lies on the triangle* (`test_the_relation_is_the_triangle`). The sum of a unit and its double is 3 on
    one triality class and 6 on the other, the mirror exchanging them. The sum of those two is the rest. Nine is the least
    modulus in which doubling runs through every unit while such sums leave the units.
62. *The commutator is every frame's reading* (`test_hilberts_father_the_film_account`). On the cycles of lengths 3ᵏ the
    trace of [X, P] vanishes exactly, the oscillator's lowest states read i to the frame's precision, and the deviations
    summed over the basis are exactly −iN.
63. *Continuity in the step* (§23; `test_continuity_lives_in_the_step_to_the_totality`). Finite descriptions are
    countable, the diagonal escapes every enumeration, and unity read to depth n is short by one unit of its last place;
    the continuum's excess exists only as the completed totality.
64. *The separation of powers* (`test_the_separation_of_powers`). Of the registered prohibitions, those sourced in the
    figure's arithmetic all forbid kinds and all the value-forbids come from the register, the medium or a companion
    record.
65. *The law and its first count* (same battery). The numbers 3 and 7 are the first two shortfalls of doubling by one
    unit and the moduli in which doubling has periods 2 and 3. The ratio 3/7 is one tick of the clock from 1/7. Nine is
    the least modulus in which the two periods close into one complete cycle.
